<p> National Post - RSS Feedhttps : **27;259;TOOLONG , 09 Jun 2020 13:59:34 +0000Scott Stinson : Amid a push for equality , the NFL 's lack of black coaches remains a glaring problemhttp : **33;288;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;323;TOOLONG ... title= " Roger Goodell during a press conference before Super Bowl LIV at Hilton Downtown . " alt= " Roger Goodell during a press conference before Super Bowl LIV at Hilton Downtown . " data-portal-copyright= " Kirby Lee/USA TODAY " **28;358;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78559602 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The National Football League altered its hiring policies last month in a bid to increase the number of non-white coaches and executives in its ranks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The changes to the Rooney Rule , which was first established 17 years ago , happened in the middle of the first major event this year that titled the world off its axis , and before the second major event to have done so , and as a result they already seem out of step with the moment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With protests across North America and Europe against racism in policing having become a greater call for an end to discrimination , and with governments and corporations insisting that they must do more to address workforce imbalances , the NFL is still left with six non-white head coaches and general managers among its 64 such positions . The revised Rooney Rule will require teams to interview more minority candidates for those jobs , as well as for senior coordinator positions . A proposal to reward teams that hired minority candidates with improved third-round draft picks was tabled . While there are now loud calls for sweeping change , this was a tweak. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What happened in the NFL last month &amp;mdash ; before the swell of public support for anti-racism initiatives , before commissioner Roger Goodell&amp;rsquo ; s surprising apology for the way the league has handled player protests against police brutality &amp;mdash ; is instructive , because it shows that as much as institutions might acknowledge their biases and a desire to overcome them , actually doing so will not come easily . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While there is no lack of industries with a paucity of non-white employees in senior positions &amp;mdash ; government , medicine , law , and certainly the media &amp;mdash ; there is no place where the disparity is quite so glaring as in the NFL . The labour pool is about 70 per cent Black , and almost two decades after the league admitted that it had a problem ensuring that those former players had the same opportunities in coaching and management as white former players ( and non-players ) , it was left admitting that the problem has persisted . Its teams are so bad at hiring non-whites to their top jobs that they considered literally bribing themselves to do it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The bizarre thing about all this is that teams in a hyper-competitive league would only stand to improve by expanding their hiring pool , and yet they still don&amp;rsquo ; t do it . One of the underpinnings of the statistical revolutions across all sports in recent years has been the recognition that one key to success is to find something valuable that other teams are undervaluing and exploit it . In baseball in was on-base percentage , in basketball it was the three-point shot , in hockey it was puck possession . The idea of hiring statisticians into management roles went from wildly controversial to routine in the space of a few seasons . Now any franchise that does not have a dedicated statistical analysis department &amp;mdash ; the dreaded computer nerds &amp;mdash ; is the outlier. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hiring more coaches and executives from the non-traditional pool offers the same potential competitive advantage to an NFL team . It&amp;rsquo ; s not unlike the manner in which the league has considered the Black quarterback . For ages they were a novelty , and even though a few had broken through and proven themselves , teams were still far more likely to draft a big white kid who could stand in the pocket and chuck the ball a mile than they were a Black quarterback , even one with a gaudy college resume . But now any list of the most productive quarterbacks in the NFL has most of the non-white starters bunched at the top : Patrick Mahomes , Russell Wilson , Lamar Jackson , DeShaun Watson , Dak Prescott . Drew Brees could be slotted in there somewhere as the white-QB torch bearer among the present elite , but that&amp;rsquo ; s about it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;388;TOOLONG ... Stinson : A big soccer match , and a suggestion that pro sports in a pandemic can still look **29;423;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;454;TOOLONG ... Stinson on COVID-19 : After years of declining interest , golf is suddenly hot **26;489;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;517;TOOLONG ... Stinson : Pro leagues are developing complicated safety plans . But will their players buy in ? &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p&gt; ... This is not to say that the league has totally adjusted its way of thinking about Black quarterbacks ; Jackson was infamously passed over by every team in the 2018 draft before Baltimore took him at the end of the first round . He was the league MVP in 2019 . But there have to be personnel departments looking at the success of the league&amp;rsquo ; s Black quarterbacks and realizing that their evaluation processes are flawed . The Chicago Bears , having passed on both Mahomes and Watson in 2017 to take Mitch Trubisky , might have destroyed the myth of the white-QB-with-potential all by themselves . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Like the crop of thriving non-white quarterbacks , why wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t there be untapped talent in the potential coaching ranks , once a team looks a little wider to find the right candidate ? Real change will come when teams realize that it will be to their benefit to overcome their traditional biases , not because a league rule forces them to conduct a couple extra interviews . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That is , obviously , true for other industries that have a deficit of minorities in leadership positions . But sports teams and leagues have a unique opportunity to lead by example , because their top jobs are so visible . Goodell&amp;rsquo ; s unexpected statement on Friday , which condemned &amp;ldquo ; racism and the systemic oppression of Black people&amp;rdquo ; was notable because it was more blunt and conciliatory than anything the NFL had previously said on the issue . We&amp;rsquo ; ll only know how serious its teams are about change if , a decade from now , they aren&amp;rsquo ; t arguing about changing the Rooney Rule again . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Postmedia News &lt;br&gt; sstinson@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; Scott StinsonTue , 09 Jun 2020 13:59:34 **45;552;TOOLONG ... Stinson : A six-minute stretch of the fourth quarter is where Super Bowl storylines went to diehttp : **37;599;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;638;TOOLONG ... title= " Kansas City Chiefs ' Patrick Mahomes in action at the Super Bowl Feb. 2 , 2020. " alt= " Kansas City Chiefs ' Patrick Mahomes in action at the Super Bowl Feb. 2 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " Mike Blake/Reuters " **28;673;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78397152 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Halfway through the fourth quarter of the &lt;a href= " https : **33;703;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Super Bowl&lt;/a&gt; , the post-game storylines were mostly written . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Patrick Mahomes , who had been lauded for two weeks as the best quarterback in the world , was having his worst game as a pro . Normally an aggressive and electric passer , he was cautious and timid and , oddly , making plain bad throws . He had been a mix of Aaron Rodgers and Lamar Jackson in the previous two playoff games . Now he was Jared Goff . Bad games happen , but having one in your first Super Bowl is like football&amp;rsquo ; s scarlet letter . Ask Andy Reid about that : Mahomes&amp;rsquo ; coach had been trying to shake the memory of his first Super Bowl , and the branding that came with it , for a decade . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kyle Shanahan and Jimmy Garoppolo , meanwhile , were busy burnishing their legacies . I can not recall a pre-Super Bowl period where a head coach was praised so much for his specific scheming abilities as Shanahan was before this one , but the San Francisco offence had indeed been mostly unstoppable . They had run the ball effectively and passed enough to keep the Chiefs off balance . Garoppolo had made one dumb throw , a first-half heave while under pressure that was intercepted , but he was otherwise accurate and poised . He had come into the game as the possible weak link for the Niners , and yet there he was , decisively outplaying Mahomes . Jimmy Montana , et cetera. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And then , in about six minutes of game time , all of that went haywire . If nothing else , the remainder of the fourth quarter proved how dumb it is to try to derive larger narratives from the results of a single game . Sometimes sports just happen . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;738;TOOLONG ... title= " Patrick Mahomes ( 15 ) celebrates after a touchdown in the fourth quarter against the San Francisco 49ers in Super Bowl LIV at Hard Rock Stadium . " alt= " Patrick Mahomes ( 15 ) celebrates after a touchdown in the fourth quarter against the San Francisco 49ers in Super Bowl LIV at Hard Rock Stadium . " data-portal-copyright= " Robert Deutsch/USA TODAY Sports " **28;773;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78397157 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; It certainly did on Sunday night . There was Mahomes , down 10 points with seven minutes left , having just badly underthrown Tyreek Hill on a catch that was overturned on review because the nose of the ball hit the ground . Facing third-and-15 , the Chiefs were one play from certain doom . Instead , Hill found open space deep down the left sideline and Mahomes launched the ball toward him . It wasn&amp;rsquo ; t a particularly good throw , as Hill had to slow down and wait for a ball that had too much air under it , but it changed everything . For everyone . That 44-yard completion set up all that followed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Chiefs were in the end zone exactly one minute later , which meant San Francisco was protecting a 20-17 lead with more than six minutes remaining . Knowing they need to make first downs to chew up time , Shanahan must have decided that he needed to throw the ball . Up to that point there had been precious little evidence that this was true ; the Niners were ripping off about six yards per carry and it sure looked like they could simply plow their way to victory , eating up time and yards awhile doing it . Instead , Shanahan called a pass on second-and-five , and Kansas City lineman Chris Jones got his meaty arms in the way to knock it down . Another incompletion followed , and all of a sudden the Chiefs were getting the ball back . Troublingly for the Niners , the formerly meek Mahomes was now feeling his oats . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;803;TOOLONG ... title= " Kansas City Chiefs ' Patrick Mahomes scores their first touchdown. " alt= " Kansas City Chiefs ' Patrick Mahomes scores their first touchdown. " data-portal-copyright= " Shannon Stapleton/Reuters " **28;838;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78397163 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Here is now the drive chart looks for that next K.C. possession : completion , completion , completion , completion , six-yard run . The Chiefs moved 65 yards in about 90 seconds and Damien Williams scored two plays later to give them a 24-20 lead . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With that , it was on Garoppolo to take over and lead a San Francisco comeback . More to the point , it was now on him to do the exact thing that the football world had spent two weeks wondering if he could do . It will be lost to history , but for a while he did . A run and two passes moved the ball 35 yards in less than a minute , and Garoppolo stood at midfield with 1:49 left and all three timeouts . All kinds of time , and all kinds of options . Here is how the drive chart looks from that point : Incompletion , incompletion , incompletion , sack . Good night . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So much for the narratives . Mahomes went full Mahomes at the end , making plays with his arm and legs and reasserting himself as the most dangerous quarterback in the game , while ensuring that Reid would never again be asked about being out-coached in a Super Bowl . Garoppolo went from tidy and effective to Rex Grossman . From that point at midfield with the game undecided , he had five incompletions , a sack and an interception . No completions . Not great ! And Shanahan , having also watched as the Patriots stormed back from 28-3 against the Falcons in the Super Bowl when he was Atlanta&amp;rsquo ; s offensive coordinator , will now have months to wonder why a genius playcaller forgets to call runs when protecting a lead . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;868;TOOLONG ... Stinson : Gerard Gallant is proof -- being NHL coach of the year does n't give you job **29;903;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;934;TOOLONG ... Stinson : Kobe Bryant and a complicated **27;969;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;998;TOOLONG ... Stinson : Major League Baseball wants the cheating scandal to go away quickly . But what if the Astros do n't hit ? &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p &g; ... It could have easily all been so different . Maybe Garoppolo&amp;rsquo ; s pass slips through the arms of Jones on second-and-five for a completion &amp;mdash ; the kind of play that happens all the time &amp;mdash ; and Shanahan is lauded today for the bold aggressiveness that kept the Niners on the march and secured the win . Maybe Mahomes and Reid are explaining this morning how their vaunted offence couldn&amp;rsquo ; t do anything until it was too late . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But , it didn&amp;rsquo ; t and they aren&amp;rsquo ; t . Sports happened . CLARY/AFP via Getty Images " **28;1589;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78396250 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;San Francisco 49ers 20 -- 31 Kansas City Chiefs &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Kansas City Chiefs have snatched the &lt;a href= " https : **33;1619;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Super Bowl&lt;/a&gt; from the San Francisco 49ers , thanks to two crucial touchdowns from Damien Williams , and a burst of magic from Patrick Mahomes when it mattered most . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As Postmedia&amp;rsquo ; s Scott Stinson put it , &amp;ldquo ; Mahomes was pretty awful , right up until he wasn&amp;rsquo ; t. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We hope you followed along below for all the play-by-play and post-game reaction , as it happened . Stinson : Tom Brady says he 's coming back to the NFL at 43 years old , and the end-of-dynasty talk is officially on holdhttp : **36;1839;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;1877;TOOLONG ... title= " New England Patriots quarterback Tom Brady ( 12 ) walks off of the field after a loss to the Tennessee Titans at Gillette Stadium . " alt= " New England Patriots quarterback Tom Brady ( 12 ) walks off of the field after a loss to the Tennessee Titans at Gillette Stadium . " data-portal-copyright= " Greg M. Cooper-USA TODAY " **28;1912;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78363567 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Like all right-thinking individuals outside of New England , I wanted the Patriots to lose on Saturday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Like just about everyone , I was also surprised that it actually happened , which helps explain why it was the desired outcome . The Patriots are the longest-running inevitability in sports , a relentless , ruthless undeniable force in a league that forces parity like no other . Every time you talked yourself into believing that the dynasty was finally at its end , there was Bill Belichick scheming his counterpart into a straitjacket and Tom Brady spreading the field on fourth-and-3 and throwing a four-yard dart to an uncovered running back . It was frustratingly predictable a decade ago &amp;mdash ; in the sense that sports are more fun when unexpected things happen &amp;mdash ; but then the pattern just kept repeating itself . At least when Thanos showed up to knock the crap out of the Avengers , they killed him in the next movie . ( Spoiler alert. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I would like to offer a grudging eulogy of the Patriots here , but alas I can not . Brady , in the style of the time , announced on social media on Wednesday that he is not done yet . In a post that , surprisingly , included a shout out to Teddy Roosevelt , the quarterback wrote that losses can happen and that people can &amp;ldquo ; learn from that failure , pick yourself up with great enthusiasm , and place yourself in the arena again . &amp;rdquo ; He said that&amp;rsquo ; s what he will do , &amp;ldquo ; because I know I still have more to prove . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;1942;TOOLONG ... title= " Tom Brady #12 of the New England Patriots throws a pass against the Tennessee Titans in the AFC Wild Card Playoff game at Gillette Stadium on January 04 , 2020 in Foxborough , Massachusetts . " alt= " Tom Brady #12 of the New England Patriots throws a pass against the Tennessee Titans in the AFC Wild Card Playoff game at Gillette Stadium on January 04 , 2020 in Foxborough , Massachusetts . " data-portal-copyright= " Maddie Meyer/Getty Images " **28;1977;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78363586 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is one of the most nonsensical things that Brady has ever said , which is something considering he sells a line of pyjamas that claim infrared technology will bring improved sleep . Brady has the six Super Bowls , a record , and the countless other remarkable accomplishments associated with New England&amp;rsquo ; s long run of dominance &amp;mdash ; the 17 AFC East crowns , the 17-year streak of 10-win seasons &amp;mdash ; and in recent seasons he has been doing this at an age that defies history , precedent and logic . Brady won the Super Bowl at 39 , lost the Super Bowl at 40 , and won it again at 41 . Every one of those seasons was unmatched by someone playing quarterback at that age . Even this year , when Brady finally started to look a little creaky in the second half of the season &amp;mdash ; perhaps time to goose the infrared in his jammies &amp;mdash ; he was still putting together a performance that was ridiculous for someone who is 42 years old . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over the last 60 years , the only comparable was Warren Moon , who was still chucking it around for the Seattle Seahawks at that age . But Moon started 10 games and threw for 1,632 yards and 11 touchdowns . Brady had 4,057 yards and 24 touchdowns . What more could Brady possible have left to prove , unless it is that he is , in fact , an age-defying alien who has taken human form for the very specific purpose of making people in Buffalo sad at least twice a year ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There is , I guess , that one thing . Brady has done all of this as Belichick has , deservedly , received a good chunk of the credit for the Patriots&amp;rsquo ; exhausting success . The quarterback&amp;rsquo ; s announcement on Wednesday did not specifically say that he intends to return to New England , and perhaps he is entertaining thoughts of a new team where he would be out from under the shadow of the coach&amp;rsquo ; s perma-scowl. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But , why risk it ? Why attempt the never-before-achieved feat of a successful 43-year-old NFL quarterback while also increasing the degree of difficulty by adding a new coach , new scheme and new teammates ? The far more likely outcome would be a Minnesota Favre or a Kansas City Montana , and years later we would all pretend that it had never happened . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;2007;TOOLONG ... title= " Tom Brady #12 of the New England Patriots walks to the sideline during the first half against the Tennessee Titans in the AFC Wild Card Playoff game at Gillette Stadium on January 04 , 2020 in Foxborough , Massachusetts . " alt= " Tom Brady #12 of the New England Patriots walks to the sideline during the first half against the Tennessee Titans in the AFC Wild Card Playoff game at Gillette Stadium on January 04 , 2020 in Foxborough , Massachusetts . " data-portal-copyright= " Adam Glanzman/Getty Images " **28;2042;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78363593 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s not like anyone would seriously claim that Brady wasn&amp;rsquo ; t an integral part of what has happened in New England . Yes , he has had backups that have performed well in the Pats&amp;rsquo ; system in spot duty , and a couple of those Super Bowls were hilariously fortuitous , and Rob Gronkowski did provide a hell of a security blanket in the later years , but it&amp;rsquo ; s also true that Brady has piled up all those wins and stats with an ever-changing cast of skill-position players . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Brady has thrown for 74 , 571 yards in his career , and New England&amp;rsquo ; s all-time leader in receiving yards is Stanley Morgan , who retired in 1989 . Of that Patriots top-10 all-time leaders in receiving yardage , and with the massive caveat that there is far more passing in the modern game , only three of them &amp;mdash ; Gronkowski , Wes Welker , and Julian Edelman &amp;mdash ; were primarily Brady guys . He&amp;rsquo ; s done all that he has done with whatever collection of fellows that Belichick has been able to cobble together . Again , what more could Brady prove ? Start throwing exclusively to fullbacks ? I have him down for 3,700 yards in 15 games because a fullback got excited and concussed him with a headbutt. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; All of which means , tragically , that we will probably see Brady in New England again . Anything else would be more interesting , but , like Brady in the five-wide set , it feels inevitable . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I hope I&amp;rsquo ; m wrong . Brady has done things that will never be matched , and maybe he will suddenly wake up one day soon and realize it . Walk away , legacy intact . There is no shame in retirement . Stinson : The NFL extends a helping hand to Colin Kaepernick . Naturally , it botches the whole thinghttp : **35;2500;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;2537;TOOLONG ... title= " In this file photo taken on September 08 , 2018 a Nike Ad featuring American football quarterback Colin Kaepernick is on diplay in New York City . " alt= " In this file photo taken on September 08 , 2018 a Nike Ad featuring American football quarterback Colin Kaepernick is on diplay in New York City . " data-portal-copyright= " ANGELA WEISS/AFP/Getty Images " **28;2572;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78299443 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; If you told me that a professional sports league had come up with a public-relations exercise that turned into a public-relations embarrassment before it had even happened , and gave me three guesses to name the outfit , I would pick the National Football League and decline the other two guesses. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And , so , Colin Kaepernick . It emerged on Tuesday , while this country was in the throes of a sports story that quickly morphed into a social-political one with a lot of ALL CAPS venting and use of modern insults like libtard and snowflake , that the former San Francisco 49ers quarterback had been invited to work out for NFL teams . It was a throwback to a story that had long ago gone quiet , one that , prior to this week , was the last time I received a flurry of poorly spelled emails that called me names and accused me of not supporting the troops . An unexpected bookend to a strange few days . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Tuesday , all 32 NFL teams received a memo that the free-agent quarterback , who was the first player to kneel during the U.S. national anthem as a protest of police brutality and systemic racism , and has been out of the league for three seasons , had been invited to a workout in Atlanta on Saturday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;blockquote class= " twitter-tweet " data-width= " 550 " data-dnt= " true " &gt;&lt;p lang= " en " dir= " ltr " &gt;I 'm just getting word from my representatives that the NFL league office reached out to them about a workout in Atlanta on Saturday . I 've been in shape and ready for this for 3 years , ca n't wait to see the head coaches and GMs on Saturday . &lt;/p&gt; -- Colin Kaepernick ( @Kaepernick7 ) &lt;a href= " https : **33;2602;TOOLONG ... 13 , **33;2637;TOOLONG &lt;script async src= " https : **33;2672;TOOLONG " charset= " utf-8 " &gt;&lt;/script&gt; &lt;p&gt; Teams could send representatives , and he would run through drills and talk to them , much in the same way that draft-eligible college players show themselves off to pro scouts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The timing of all this is extremely odd . Kaepernick is 32 years old and has not played a snap in the NFL since 2016 , when he had 18 total touchdowns and four interceptions , and one win in 11 starts , for an awful 49ers team . A lawsuit that alleged the NFL&amp;rsquo ; s owners had colluded to keep him out of the league was settled in February . This is Week 11 of a 17-week regular season , far from an ideal time to even consider trying to integrate a player into a team&amp;rsquo ; s offensive system at its most complicated position . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And yet Kaepernick , who has mostly kept quiet during his exile , probably to the detriment of his career , said he was ready for the opportunity to show he could still play . &amp;ldquo ; Can&amp;rsquo ; t wait to see the head coaches and GMs on Saturday , &amp;rdquo ; he posted on Twitter . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;2707;TOOLONG ... title= " Colin Kaepernick #7 of the San Francisco 49ers and Eli Harold #58 kneel during the National Anthem prior to the game against the Atlanta Falcons at the Georgia Dome on December 18 , 2016 in Atlanta , Georgia . " alt= " Colin Kaepernick #7 of the San Francisco 49ers and Eli Harold #58 kneel during the National Anthem prior to the game against the Atlanta Falcons at the Georgia Dome on December 18 , 2016 in Atlanta , Georgia . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;2742;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78299445 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yes , well , about that . Saturday is about the dumbest possible day to hold such a workout , as teams are either travelling to road games or in final preparations to play on Sunday . This seems like the kind of thing the NFL would have known on its own , but then again this is the league that , when investigating allegations of violence committed by one of its players , sometimes forgets to ask if there is security-camera footage available . ESPN has reported that Kapernick&amp;rsquo ; s representatives asked to have the workout shifted to Tuesday , but were refused . They also asked to have it moved to the following Saturday , but were refused that , too . Teams with a possible interest in seeing what kind of football condition he is in will have to send whomever they can find , on short notice , to Atlanta on Saturday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kaepernick does not know who might be there . No one knows why the league is doing this now , or why it is insisting on hewing to an inconvenient schedule . Why , it is almost as if the NFL wants to do the bare minimum to show that Kaepernick was afforded the opportunity to resume his career , but not have him actually resume it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;2772;TOOLONG ... podcast : Don Cherry firing brings rage , but hockey moved on long ago&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;2807;TOOLONG ... Stinson : Did Don Cherry just misspeak , or has he laid bare Canada 's divisions ? &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt; ... href= " https : **33;2842;TOOLONG ... Stinson : The Houston Astros are the team of data ( that failed them in the end ) &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p &g; ... Even that , though , makes little sense . To the extent that it was once an obvious embarrassment that the NFL&amp;rsquo ; s teams , who all insist that winning is everything , were too afraid to sign a player who might help them win at the game&amp;rsquo ; s most important position , that sentiment has faded . The longer he was out of the game , the easier it was to justify why he was not being given a chance . At any point in the last three seasons there have been dozens of quarterbacks on NFL rosters with far less proven ability than Kaepernick , and injury churn has resulted in all kinds of random players being handed starting jobs , and yet he has been out there , the third rail that no team has been willing to touch . Once the Buffalo Bills signed 35-year-old Derek Anderson , who had been retired , to fill a quarterback need last season , it seemed obvious that Kaepernick was done . Coaches would say they were happy with their quarterback situation , and that situation would include an undrafted rookie and a 42-year-old on his sixth team . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; NFL commissioner Roger Goodell has always said , whenever the Kaepernick question was raised , that it is up to teams to make decisions about players . He would say that front offices are always trying to make their teams better , a statement that can be filed right next to his suggestions that it was a good idea for the Chargers to leave San Diego . And now , for reasons only the NFL knows , they extend an offer to help Kaepernick return to the league that unofficially banished him . It doesn&amp;rsquo ; t seem like a genuine offer , and so far it has only served to fan the embers of a long-since-cooled controversy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Maybe Goodell just figured it had been too long since the league had done something incompetent , and he was feeling out of sorts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Postmedia News&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " **29;2877;TOOLONG " **36;2908;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Scott StinsonThu , 14 Nov 2019 22:04:48 **45;2946;TOOLONG ... Stinson : The XFL has signed its first player , and it sure seems like good news for the CFLhttp : **37;2993;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;3032;TOOLONG ... title= " Landry Jones . Sound familiar ? " alt= " Landry Jones . Sound familiar ? " data-portal-copyright= " Wikipedia " **28;3067;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78188337 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canadian Football League received an unexpected bit of good news this week , and that news was : Landry Jones . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I will admit that when the email landed that said Landry Jones was the first player to ever sign with the reincarnated XFL , my first thought was &amp;ldquo ; Oh ? I&amp;rsquo ; ve heard of that guy . &amp;rdquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s a low bar , admittedly , but signing players with at least a dash of NFL pedigree would seem to give the XFL at least a little in the way of early-stage credibility . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And then I took a closer look and changed my mind . I remembered Jones as a former collegiate star and the understudy to Ben Roethlisberger with the Pittsburgh Steelers . Both those things are true . But he is also 30 years old and did not play at all in the NFL last season . Nor will he , evidently , in this coming season . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Steelers cut him on the eve of last season , and Jacksonville and Oakland have signed him and then released him in the months since . And so , in five NFL seasons , he made five starts . He threw for 1,310 yards , or a good Pat Mahomes month , with eight touchdowns and seven interceptions . The last time he was a regular starter was seven years ago , in Oklahoma , and by the time the XFL debuts next spring he will not have played a game that counted for about two years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Keep in mind , here , that the XFL made a point of announcing on Thursday : We got Landry Jones ! First player in league history ! ( Double finger-guns. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Perhaps all they were hoping for was the same reaction across the football world that I had : hey , I have heard of him . It&amp;rsquo ; s a weird way to build excitement in your fledgling league . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The CFL , of course , is the furthest thing from fledgling . Second-oldest championship trophy in North American pro sports , and all that . As recently as last fall , the prospect of two planned spring football leagues in the United States loomed as some degree of a competitive threat . The would-be Alliance of American Football and the reconstituted XFL each had wealthy , connected people involved and they were promising base salaries considerably higher than those of the CFL , and a schedule that fit better with players who still harboured thoughts of an NFL career . Play for one of the new leagues in the spring , perform well , and go straight into an NFL camp . The CFL has seen off many such threats over the decades , but if one ever does stick , it would represent something of a crisis for the Canadian league&amp;rsquo ; s talent base . Certain wiseacre columnists &amp;mdash ; whistles idly , shuffles feet &amp;mdash ; wondered at the Grey Cup last year if either the AAF or the XFL would be the one to do it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;3097;TOOLONG ... title= " Seattle Seahawks former quarterback and Seattle XFL franchise coach Jim Zorn ( left ) talks with Denver Broncos quarterback Joe Flacco ( 5 ) before a game at CenturyLink Field . " alt= " Seattle Seahawks former quarterback and Seattle XFL franchise coach Jim Zorn ( left ) talks with Denver Broncos quarterback Joe Flacco ( 5 ) before a game at CenturyLink Field . " data-portal-copyright= " Kirby Lee-USA TODAY Sports " **28;3132;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78188348 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; But the AAF came and went before it even completed a season , with Carolina Hurricanes owner Tom Dundon bizarrely arriving to be its saviour and then its executioner in a matter of weeks . Now it is the XFL&amp;rsquo ; s turn . The league went through a series of head-coach announcements already , with most of the names recognizable to football fans : Bob Stoops , June Jones , Marc Trestman and , er , Jim Zorn . But finding coaches was always going to be the easy part , as there are far more of them than there are high-profile professional coaching jobs . Finding players is the greater challenge , and announcing Landry Jones as the marquee first signing only underscores that point . The XFL says it plans to sign seven more &amp;ldquo ; leading&amp;rdquo ; quarterbacks before a draft in October &amp;mdash ; confusingly , players sign with the league first , and then enter a draft to find out where they will play &amp;mdash ; and it expects that a good chunk of its talent pool will be filled by those players who are among the final cuts at NFL camps over the next few weeks . But many such players would rather try to hang on and land on a different NFL team , or even its practice squad , before signing up with Vince McMahon&amp;rsquo ; s latest pet project . Jones is the only example because he&amp;rsquo ; s the only guy signed , but he&amp;rsquo ; s not an up-and-comer trying to use the XFL as a springboard to the NFL , he&amp;rsquo ; s someone who likely realized that his NFL days are over and so he will make some money this way . Feel the excitement ! &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;3162;TOOLONG ... Stinson : A rental car robbery cost A-Rod $500K . Potential XFL roster-building issues aside , what the AAF experience proved , again , is that these ventures are extremely costly . Even with decent early television ratings and games in a few venues that were moderately well-attended , the AAF went through money like poop through a goose . McMahon seems willing to suffer steep losses to see the XFL through the lean years , but that was supposed to be the idea behind Dundon&amp;rsquo ; s AAF intervention , too . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; None of this is to say that it is all sunshine and rainbows in the CFL . The Vancouver , Montreal and Toronto franchises are all enveloped in various states of crisis , and the new CFL 2.0 positioning as a global aggregator of football talent has not been a rousing success out of the gate , unless you put an awful lot of weight on the fact that a fellow from France has made two ( 2 ) special-teams tackles for the Edmonton Eskimos . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But , as for the CFL&amp;rsquo ; s looming competitive threats ? Stinson : We owe it to ourselves to root for the Chargers against the Patriots this Sundayhttp : **34;3370;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;3406;TOOLONG ... title= " New England Patriots head coach Bill Belichick faces reporters before an NFL football practice , Wednesday , Jan. 9 , 2019 , in Foxborough , Mass. " data-portal-copyright= " Steven Senne/AP Photo " **28;3441;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 77845054 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; I can not be the only person who watched the final minutes of the Ravens-Chargers game on Sunday with a gnawing , growing feeling of dread . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It wasn&amp;rsquo ; t because I had any strong feelings toward the Ravens or Chargers specifically , or any particular dislike of either . No , it was because , as Baltimore , after being utterly somnambulant for the first 50 minutes , was now scrambling around and still alive , I kept having the same thought : good God , the Patriots are going to go to the Super Bowl again . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I say I can not be the only person who watched the game this way with some confidence , because after thinking I was the only one torturing myself , I have since heard from several who said the same thing . With Baltimore and rookie quarterback Lamar Jackson looking hopeless for most of that game , it looked like a strong Chargers team would be travelling to Foxboro this weekend for a date with New England . This was good : no one , outside of Pats fans , wants to see them advance again . The former San Diegos would at least have a good shot at knocking them off . A miracle Baltimore comeback , though , would have been a disaster . It had been obvious through the first three quarters of that game that a smart team had figured out how to stop the Ravens&amp;rsquo ; run-heavy offence ; Bill Belichick would do the exact same thing and he wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t be dumb enough , like the Chargers , to let the Ravens hang around at the end . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Patriots would have been a lock to return to the AFC Championship . And then , Belichick versus Andy Reid and the Chiefs at Arrowhead ? Or Belichick versus Frank Reich in Foxboro ? Neither scenario is the least bit pleasing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;3471;TOOLONG ... title= " In this Dec. 30 , 2018 , file photo , Kansas City Chiefs head coach Andy Reid gestures during the first half of an NFL game against the Oakland Raiders , in Kansas City , Mo . " alt= " In this Dec. 30 , 2018 , file photo , Kansas City Chiefs head coach Andy Reid gestures during the first half of an NFL game against the Oakland Raiders , in Kansas City , Mo . " data-portal-copyright= " Ed Zurga/AP Photo " **28;3506;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 77845126 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Both scenarios remain , of course , entirely possible . Which is why we , as a society , owe it to ourselves to root for the Chargers this Sunday . These are fractured times , with polarized electorates , and fights over walls and pipelines and the future of our planet and many other weighty things . But surely this is something we can all rally around , if even just for about three hours on a weekend afternoon : please , Los Angeles Chargers , do this for us. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I&amp;rsquo ; m not sure why , exactly , the prospect of another deep New England run seems quite so unappealing . Sure , there is the fact that the Patriots have been to one million Super Bowls ( approximately ) in an era when it is supposed to be very hard to be consistently good . But this has been the case for a long time now . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And yes , I admit to holding a couple of personal grudges here . I&amp;rsquo ; m a lapsed Buffalo Bills fan , but the scars from all the beatings over the years remain . And in terms of furious press-box rewrites , few experiences have been as unpleasant as Super Bowl LI , when I had a very nice column about the Atlanta win pretty much completed by halftime before the Patriots ruined what should have been a comfortable second-half experience . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But it is more than that . This has felt at times like a watershed NFL season , where the league that constantly scores public-relations own goals suddenly found itself with an offensive explosion keyed by young stars like Patrick Mahomes and Jared Goff . The Monday night shootout between those two in November was great fun , the best game in years , and there was no one named Brady or Manning anywhere near it . We have , for a long time now , wondered how the NFL would transition from the old-warrior era of Brady and the Mannings and Big Ben , and now here were these young guys flinging it all over the place . Football ! &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;3536;TOOLONG ... title= " In this Dec. 30 , 2018 , file photo , Kansas City Chiefs quarterback Patrick Mahomes ( 15 ) throws a touchdown pass to wide receiver Demarcus Robinson , unseen , during the second half of an NFL game against the Oakland Raiders , in Kansas City , Mo . " alt= " In this Dec. 30 , 2018 , file photo , Kansas City Chiefs quarterback Patrick Mahomes ( 15 ) throws a touchdown pass to wide receiver Demarcus Robinson , unseen , during the second half of an NFL game against the Oakland Raiders , in Kansas City , Mo . " data-portal-copyright= " Charlie Riedel/AP Photo " **28;3571;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 77845129 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; And yet , hovering around in the background all season have been the Pats , the super villain that won&amp;rsquo ; t die even though it has been blown up and dropped off a cliff and buried under a collapsed skyscraper . Even with all the changing-of-the-guard stuff happening , there was 41-year-old Brady , allegedly feuding with his coach but still throwing to a collection of small receivers and an increasingly creaky Rob Gronkowski , who now &lt;a href= " https : //www.youtube.com/watch ? v=DphiN4w ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;lumbers around like an elephant&lt;/a&gt; that has been hit with a tranquilizer dart but will still haul in the desperation pass on third-and-18 with two defenders hanging off him . Every time it looked like New England had lost enough games to ensure that it would not have a first-round bye in the playoffs , one of the Texans , Chiefs or Chargers would throw up on themselves to bring the Pats back in line for a top-two seed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;3601;TOOLONG ... Stinson : With NHL expansion to Seattle , a second Quebec team remains pure **28;3706;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt; ... And now , here we are . Nothing about this Patriots season suggests that they should get through the next two weeks to make their third straight Super Bowl and ninth in the Belichick era . The defence has been soft , Brady appears human , in the sense that he actually ages over time , and he has a collection of iffy weapons . When the Pats traded for Josh Gordon , it was easy to imagine a guy who had barely played since 2013 stepping in to become Brady&amp;rsquo ; s key big-game target , the receiver who would go for 175 yards and two TDs as Reid was staring at his play card in confusion in the AFC title game . But even that didn&amp;rsquo ; t work out , Belichick turning to his old bag of tricks and this time coming up empty . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Is it a sign that this is finally it ? Is the most remarkable dynasty in sports about to be finished off by the ravages of time , and also the salary cap ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I imagine most anyone who watches football would like to think so , but I&amp;rsquo ; m not quite ready to believe it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Postmedia News&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Scott StinsonThu , 10 Jan 2019 21:30:29 **45;3736;TOOLONG ... teams with quarterback-sized holes , the NFL has moved on without Colin Kaepernickhttp : **30;3783;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;3815;TOOLONG ... title= " Terrell Suggs #55 of the Baltimore Ravens sacks quarterback Nathan Peterman #2 of the Buffalo Bills in the second quarter at M&amp;amp ; T Bank Stadium on September 9 , 2018 in Baltimore , Maryland . Injuries had forced the Buffalo Bills to turn to their third-stringer mid-game . In went Billy Joe Hobert , and as he scrambled around hopelessly , it looked like he didn&amp;rsquo ; t know the plays . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He didn&amp;rsquo ; t know the plays . Hobert , after a two-interception performance that included several other throws where the ball was nowhere near a Bills receiver , admitted afterward that his familiarity with the playbook was not exactly comprehensive . The dog ate his homework , or something . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Bills promptly released him . I thought about Hobert when Buffalo was forced to turn again to Nathan Peterman after rookie Josh Allen was hurt against the Texans . Peterman reprised his spectacularly bad Week 1 performance with another two-pick special , including one returned for a touchdown that gave Houston the win . He has now appeared in six games and thrown an interception on 13 per cent of his pass attempts . That is more than double the career interception rate of Ryan Leaf . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;3880;TOOLONG ... title= " Terrell Suggs #55 of the Baltimore Ravens sacks quarterback Nathan Peterman #2 of the Buffalo Bills in the second quarter at M&amp;T Bank Stadium on September 9 , 2018 in Baltimore , Maryland . They were so sure of this fact that they traded away A.J. McCarron in the pre-season , even though they had just signed him to a two-year deal to be the veteran who kept the QB seat warm while Allen got up to speed . And yet Peterman was objectively worse than the guy who once didn&amp;rsquo ; t read the playbook. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; All of which is to say , if the Peterman clip reel does not feature prominently in Colin Kaepernick&amp;rsquo ; s collusion case against the NFL , then his lawyers need to watch more tape . He threw three interceptions in Buffalo&amp;rsquo ; s loss to the Colts on Sunday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Don&amp;rsquo ; t worry , this is isn&amp;rsquo ; t about to be another column that argues at length about why Kaepernick should be on an NFL roster . The calculus for that hasn&amp;rsquo ; t changed over the two seasons during which he has been avoided like the plague by many NFL teams with a quarterback-sized hole on their roster . It was obvious last fall that the reasons for his absence from the league went beyond his ability to play football , and it remains obvious today . Consider that three teams &amp;mdash ; Buffalo , Arizona and Cleveland &amp;mdash ; entered the season with rookie quarterbacks that they didn&amp;rsquo ; t think were ready to start , so they left the teams in the hands of various veteran journeymen . Those quarterbacks , Peterman , Sam Bradford , and Tyrod Taylor , ranged from ineffective to cringe-inducing , and so the rookies were thrown in . It looks increasingly like all of them could have used more seasoning on the bench . If Kaepernick was just a guy , and not the face of a protest movement , does anyone seriously doubt that one of those teams wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t have signed a veteran who took his team to the Super Bowl to be the stand-in while the rookies held a clipboard for a year ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;3945;TOOLONG ... title= " In this Dec. 11 , 2016 , file photo , then-San Francisco 49ers quarterback Colin Kaepernick stands in the bench area during the second half of the team 's NFL football game against the New York Jets in Santa Clara , Calif. He has utterly Bortlesed . The Jags turned to Cody Kessler after they benched Bortles on Sunday . Kessler , who had appeared in 11 career games and lost 11 of them , is now winless in 12. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Kaepernick story this season is not that some teams should have signed him , because , well , duh , but that it is increasingly clear that the NFL has totally moved on without him . The protests that he began three seasons ago have again dwindled to only a handful of players . The only times the issue has flared has been when someone other than the players poked the smouldering embers : Donald Trump last fall and the owners this past spring , when they unilaterally changed the rules to require any player on the field to stand during the national anthem . The owners , having backed down from that plan when the players complained about it , now appear likely to not bring it up again . The issue has died down , no one is talking about respecting the anthem , and even antagonists like Eric Reid and Malcolm Jenkins are fighting among themselves , which no doubt makes some of the owners positively giddy . Trump hasn&amp;rsquo ; t even complained about the NFL for months . Maybe he just appreciates the explosion in offence . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What team would bring Kaepernick into this environment ? Sure , he&amp;rsquo ; s a proven NFL quarterback with playoff experience and he might be able to put up spectacular numbers in a league that has made playing the position easier than it ever has been before . He hasn&amp;rsquo ; t forgotten his helmet in the locker room , or fumbled after running into his lineman&amp;rsquo ; s butt , or posted any racist things on Twitter ( recently . ) He has been decidedly not awful , and early in this Bills season , that will have to do . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Buffalo is second-last in the NFL in points scored , and tied for last in points allowed . Through the first six quarters of this season the Bills were outscored 75-9 , at which point cornerback Vontae Davis retired . It was the football equivalent of a blind date in which one of the parties leaves the table to use the bathroom and then bolts before the entrees arrive . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The fan base must be having similar thoughts . I say this as someone who was a Bills fan for a long time and then realized that I had stopped caring about the team : being a lapsed Bills fan is a lot more enjoyable . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If anything , the first two weeks of this season have added some important context to the franchise&amp;rsquo ; s beleaguered history . When the team got off to an improbable 5-2 start last season under new coach Sean McDermott , it was all very unlike them . Then they lost a couple of games and the coach decided to start Nathan Peterman over Tyrod Taylor at quarterback and that was VERY like them . Peterman , a fifth-round draft pick , threw five picks in a blowout loss to the Chargers and it looked like the Bills had blown a chance to return to the playoffs for the first time in two decades because McDermott had decided to swap the mediocre quarterback for an awful one . But , wait ! The Bills scuffled to a 9-7 record , which looked like it would leave them just out of the playoffs ( very Billsy ) , and then they were saved by a last-gasp touchdown in Week 17 from Andy Dalton and the Cincinnati Bengals , which knocked the Ravens out of the post-season picture and allowed the Bills in. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This moment of unrestrained joy was very unfamiliar to Bills fans , but now we see it for what it was : false hope . Gifted a return to the playoffs for the first time since 1999 , the Bills promptly scored three whole points in a loss to Jacksonville , and then McDermott and general manager Brandon Beane apparently remembered that they actually had planned to be bad last season so they went right back to that . Instead of building on the unexpected playoff appearance , the Bills traded Taylor to Cleveland , signed career backup A.J. McCarron to be the stop-gap quarterback and made a bunch of trades to get high enough in last spring&amp;rsquo ; s draft to nab a quarterback of the future , which turned out to be Allen . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Except McCarron didn&amp;rsquo ; t make it out of training camp , and was traded to Oakland , which led to Week 1 starter Nathan Peterman , which led to a rare passer rating of 0.0 . To be fair to McDermott , it&amp;rsquo ; s not like there was evidence of Peterman completely embarrassing himself as a starter , except for that other time when he was the starter . Only 12 times in the past 20 years has a quarterback with at least 10 attempts managed the bagel rating , and only two of those 12 threw for fewer yards than Peterman&amp;rsquo ; s 24 in the opener : Joey Harrington and Ryan Leaf . So , not great company . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;4396;TOOLONG ... title= " So far this season , Buffalo Bills quarterback Josh Allen has been unquestionably the best thing about a very bad team . The Bills are 17-point underdogs to the Vikings , which is verging into Alabama-Troy territory . Buffalo probably won&amp;rsquo ; t be favoured in a game until Week 14 at home to the Jets , if that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Again , they made the playoffs last season . The short answer is that the Bills were awful last season , too , especially late , but they survived thanks to timely turnovers and , incredibly , Andy Dalton. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They inadvertently set the rebuild back by a year , and gave their fans a taste of the good life , before cold reality has slapped them in the face again . It is all , yes , very Billsy. &lt;/p&gt; Scott StinsonTue , 18 Sep 2018 01:48:44 **45;4461;TOOLONG ... fault Le'Veon Bell for holding out to get paid in a league that incentivizes disloyaltyhttp : **30;4508;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;4540;TOOLONG ... title= " In this Jan. 14 file photo , Pittsburgh Steelers running back Le'Veon Bell is shown during a playoff game against the Jacksonville Jaguars. " alt= " In this Jan. 14 file photo , Pittsburgh Steelers running back Le'Veon Bell is shown during a playoff game against the Jacksonville Jaguars. " data-portal-copyright= " Kevin C. Cox " **28;4575;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 77543230 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Former athletes tend not to like it when current athletes exercise power that the retired players didn&amp;rsquo ; t know they had . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Witness Cris Carter on the subject of Le&amp;rsquo ; Veon Bell and his impasse with the Pittsburgh Steelers : &amp;ldquo ; Either sign the contract and come in or just shut up . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was a mild surprise , then , to see another Hall of Famer , Emmitt Smith , give his thoughts on the Bell situation , and specifically the criticism he is suddenly taking from his Steelers teammates. &amp;ldquo ; Shame on them , &amp;rdquo ; Smith said on TSN . Oh ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Smith , though , knows what of he speaks . Coming off a Super Bowl win with the Dallas Cowboys in 1992 , he held out during training camp of the 1993 season and didn&amp;rsquo ; t come back until his team was 0-2 and he had Jerry Jones right by his sensitive parts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The NFL salary cap came in the next season , and the leverage that Smith enjoyed in his holdout was largely erased . Player salaries have escalated over the past 25 years &amp;mdash ; the four-year contract that Smith signed after his holdout paid him less than running back Todd Gurley will make this season alone &amp;mdash ; but even that hasn&amp;rsquo ; t kept pace with the huge increases in NFL revenues , profits and franchise values . Jerry Richardson bought the Carolina Panthers for US$200 million in 1993 , and he just sold them for US$2.2 billion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;4605;TOOLONG ... title= " In this Jan. 14 file photo , Pittsburgh Steelers running back Le'Veon Bell is shown during a playoff game against the Jacksonville Jaguars. " alt= " In this Jan. 14 file photo , Pittsburgh Steelers running back Le'Veon Bell is shown during a playoff game against the Jacksonville Jaguars. " data-portal-copyright= " Kevin C. Cox " **28;4640;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 77543230 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Those numbers alone don&amp;rsquo ; t begin to explain the way NFL players have been impacted by the salary cap . Because their contracts aren&amp;rsquo ; t guaranteed , they are routinely cut when teams realize that they can get similar production from a younger , cheaper player . Every off-season brings stories of productive players who are cast aside for reasons related to the salary cap &amp;mdash ; an instrument entirely brought in to artificially suppress salaries . Bill Belichick alone goes through players like a wheat thresher and he is generally lauded for his willingness to be nakedly disloyal . The only protection from injury a player has in this system is whatever guaranteed money they can get written into their deal ; they can be owed tens of millions in salaries on paper but see that vanish the moment they blow out a knee . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The NFL system doesn&amp;rsquo ; t just allow disloyalty to a player from management ; it all but demands it . More recent CBA changes that suppressed the cost of rookie deals have led teams to roster more younger players at the expense of middle-tier players &amp;mdash ; the kinds of guys who used to make more than rookies but much less than stars . Now , once a serviceable veteran has outlived his entry-level deal he almost immediately becomes more of a burden than an asset . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That this system has developed over the period of years where the physical risks of playing football have become much better understood only makes it that much more incongruous . Players serve at the mercy of the teams to which they give their few productive football years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And now here comes Le&amp;rsquo ; Veon Bell , trying to squeeze whatever leverage he has out of his contract stalemate , and his own teammates blast him . Centre Maurkice Pouncey called him &amp;ldquo ; honestly , a little selfish&amp;rdquo ; and guard Ramon Foster said Bell was &amp;ldquo ; a guy who doesn&amp;rsquo ; t give a damn . &amp;rdquo ; Bell has been getting the selfish tag for months from some Steelers fans . This is now the ingrained attitude in football , and to an extent in all sports : teams can do whatever they want in the name of sound business , but athletes should shut up and play . Be grateful for the opportunity , you overpaid jerk , says the guy who just had several $15 beers at the NFL stadium that was built with hundreds of millions in public subsidies for the billionaire owner . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;4670;TOOLONG ... Malcolm Jenkins became the new face of the NFL 's protest **44;4705;TOOLONG href= " https : **33;4751;TOOLONG ... new Colin Kaepernick ad is set to air on the NFL 's opening **40;4786;TOOLONG &lt; ... Bell , who has averaged more yards from scrimmage per game than any running back in history over his first five seasons , is , at 26 years old , trying to maximize the amount of guaranteed money he will get in what would surely be his only big-dollar multi-year deal . When the Steelers could not sign him to such a deal &amp;mdash ; he reportedly turned down a US$70-million contract with US$33 million guaranteed &amp;mdash ; they used salary cap rules to offer him a one-year deal worth about US$14.5 million . They did the same thing last year . As it did then , the one-year deal would give him zero protection against injury , at a position where NFL careers are notoriously short . If the Steelers were only following CBA rules by offering Bell the one-year deal , why should Bell be vilified for following the same rules ? ( He is allowed to hold out for as many as 10 games without losing a year of service. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That fans gripe about holdouts isn&amp;rsquo ; t surprising , but you&amp;rsquo ; d think players would manage a little solidarity . NFL players in particular often go googly-eyed when they see what athletes are paid in other major leagues , and with good reason . The $33 million that the Steelers offered Bell in guaranteed money matches exactly what the Toronto Blue Jays gave Kendrys Morales , at 32 years old , to be a designated hitter for three seasons . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even amid controversies , the NFL continues to book record profits , with estimates as high as US$15 billion for the 2017 season . It does this thanks to a system that fosters ruthlessness . Good for the player who tries a little of that himself . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;em&gt;Email : &lt;a href= " **29;4828;TOOLONG " **36;4859;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a class= " twitter-follow-button " href= " https : **26;4897;TOOLONG " **38;4925;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Scott StinsonThu , 06 Sep 2018 16:34:04 **45;4965;TOOLONG ... stands with Colin Kaepernick in bet that outcry from Trump and his allies wo n't cost them businesshttp : **32;5012;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;5046;TOOLONG ... title= " In this Sept. 12 , 2016 file photo , San Francisco 49ers quarterback Colin Kaepernick kneels during the national anthem before a game against the Los Angeles Rams. It , and the National Football League in particular , are such a significant part of the culture in North America that even this has been largely overlooked . Loss of cognitive function , you say ? Everyone from Donald Trump to talk-show hosts to random Twitter eggs has insisted that players who &amp;ldquo ; disrespect&amp;rdquo ; the anthem &amp;mdash ; their word , not mine &amp;mdash ; will cause fans to flee football by the millions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The actual evidence of this happening has not materialized . Television ratings have dropped in recent seasons , but relative to the steep declines across broadcast networks , football has done quite well . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And now the NFL , thanks to the actions of one of its major corporate sponsors , finds itself with the anthem controversy enflamed &amp;mdash ; in some cases literally this time &amp;mdash ; again . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;5111;TOOLONG ... title= " In this Sept. 12 , 2016 file photo , San Francisco 49ers quarterback Colin Kaepernick kneels during the national anthem before a game against the Los Angeles Rams. " data-portal-copyright= " Marcio Jose Sanchez " **28;5146;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 77538838 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; It all leads to an existential question , one that isn&amp;rsquo ; t just about football but is about America in 2018 . Everyone knows that country is sharply divided right now . But is it a 50-50 split between those who still consider themselves proudly pro-Trump and those who do not , or is the rabidly pro-Trump slice of the pie smaller than that ? Are the people who work themselves into a lather about kneeling football players really representative of a large swath of the population , or are they more of a vocal rump ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nike , it would seem , is placing a very large bet on the latter . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nothing that has unfolded since Nike unveiled its new ad , with Kaepernick as the centrepiece , could have been a surprise to the executives at the sporting-goods giant . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;blockquote class= " twitter-tweet " data-width= " 550 " data-dnt= " true " &gt;&lt;p lang= " en " dir= " ltr " &gt;Believe in something , even if it means sacrificing everything . &lt;a href= " https : **30;5176;TOOLONG ? sr ... &lt;a href= " https : //t.co/SRWkMIDdaO " &gt;pic.twitter ... -- Colin Kaepernick ( @Kaepernick7 ) &lt;a href= " https : **33;5208;TOOLONG ... 3 , **33;5243;TOOLONG &lt;script async src= " https : **33;5278;TOOLONG " charset= " utf-8 " &gt;&lt;/script&gt; &lt;p&gt; The social media outcry , with the burning of Nike shoes and socks and the dreaded boycott hashtag , were as inevitable as the Buffalo Bills having problems at quarterback . It does make you wonder what would have happened if it was , say , Ford that put the former 49ers quarterback in their ads . Would the people burning their Air Jordans be taking a sledgehammer to their F-150s ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And so , there have been the stories about the drop in the company&amp;rsquo ; s stock and the Twitter posts of people furiously carving the Nike swoosh off their garments , presumably while humming The Star-Spangled Banner as a majestic bald eagle soars overhead . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s the kind of thing that brands &amp;mdash ; sorry , #brands &amp;mdash ; generally try to avoid . Every time some airline falls into a social media boycott pit , as airlines tend to do since they are singularly adept at angering their customers , the company scrambles to apologize and stem the online tide . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What&amp;rsquo ; s striking in the Nike case is that the marketers brought this on themselves . Consider that the NFL , multibillion-dollar business controlled by a bunch of insanely wealthy individuals , has repeatedly been spooked by the whole idea of players protesting during the anthem . The league rewrote its rules in the spring to effectively ban kneeling , only to relent when the players insisted that they had a right to be consulted on such changes . Various reports , from ESPN and other outlets , have said over the past year that many NFL owners have been terrified of losing business over the anthem-related controversies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;5313;TOOLONG ... And , of course , Kaepernick himself remains out of a job for the second straight season , even though he is plainly good enough to be on a NFL roster . That&amp;rsquo ; s the crux of the Nike ad , in fact , which features his face and the words &amp;ldquo ; Believe in something . Even if it means sacrificing everything . &amp;rdquo ; The thing he sacrificed , it turned out , was his career . None of the NFL&amp;rsquo ; s 32 teams have offered him a job since he became the face of the player protests , this despite a resume that included playoff success at the game&amp;rsquo ; s most important position . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As has been pointed out many times since Kaepernick launched a lawsuit that alleges the NFL&amp;rsquo ; s owners colluded to keep him out of the league , every week of games provides a blooper reel of plays from actual employed quarterbacks that makes a convincing case for the plaintiff . But the teams would rather give jobs to luminaries like Cody Kessler , Nathan Peterman and Jeff Driskell ( note : actual people ! ) than risk any part of a potential Kaepernick backlash . The anthem issue worries the NFL enough that ESPN , under a new president , has assured it that it won&amp;rsquo ; t show the anthem as part of its Monday night broadcasts , lest anyone be caught kneeling or raising a fist or something else that could spark a presidential tweetstorm. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And yet here comes Nike , which sells a range of fungible products and is much more likely to be boycotted than a football league , to make a public stand with the guy the NFL doesn&amp;rsquo ; t want . To bet that there is more to be gained in the long term by absorbing the blast from Trump and his allies in the short term . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nike , hardly a paragon of corporate virtue , must have researched and focus grouped this move to death . I am curious to know what it told them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;em&gt;Email : &lt;a href= " **29;5463;TOOLONG " **36;5494;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **26;5532;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " **38;5560;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Scott StinsonWed , 05 Sep 2018 13:03:58 **45;5600;TOOLONG ... Twitter fingers , protesters ' resolve ensure NFL has zero chance of making anthem issue go awayhttp : **36;5647;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;5685;TOOLONG ... title= " Miami Dolphins defensive end Robert Quinn raises his right fist during the singing of the national anthem , before the a pre-season game against the Tampa Bay Buccaneers on Aug. 9. " alt= " Miami Dolphins defensive end Robert Quinn raises his right fist during the singing of the national anthem , before the a pre-season game against the Tampa Bay Buccaneers on Aug. 9. " data-portal-copyright= " Wilfredo Lee " **28;5720;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 77508624 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Whichever poor soul in the NFL head offices is tasked with monitoring the Twitter feed of the President of the United States must have had a good Friday morning . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Donald Trump had not , by lunchtime , blasted the NFL or any of its players for a SAD lack of PATRIOTISM or any such thing . For the league , that counts as a win . With a slate of NFL pre-season games having taken place on Thursday night , and with a handful of players choosing to stay in the tunnel under the stands rather than come out and stand at attention during the national anthem , conditions were ripe for another presidential blast . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But then again , it is only a matter of time . If anything has become clear during this latest round of controversy over NFL players , social-justice protests and The Star-Spangled Banner , it&amp;rsquo ; s that there is zero chance the league will be able to make it completely go away . And , for a league that so often richly deserves to be embarrassed , it looks good on them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trump is bound to repeat his criticism of protesting players , and his demand that they be suspended or fired , because he knows it plays well with a segment of his supporters . And the players who have made a point thus far of taking some sort of action during the anthem , whether by kneeling , raising a fist or staying in the locker room , are going to continue to do so , for different reasons . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;5750;TOOLONG ... title= " Miami Dolphins defensive end Robert Quinn raises his right fist during the singing of the national anthem , before the a pre-season game against the Tampa Bay Buccaneers on Aug. 9. " alt= " Miami Dolphins defensive end Robert Quinn raises his right fist during the singing of the national anthem , before the a pre-season game against the Tampa Bay Buccaneers on Aug. 9. " data-portal-copyright= " Wilfredo Lee " **28;5785;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 77508624 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some will want to draw attention to racial injustice issues , which were the impetus for the original Colin Kaepernick protests in the fall of 2016 . Some will push back largely because the league&amp;rsquo ; s owners arbitrarily changed the rules around the anthem this spring ; those rules are on hold while the league and the players&amp;rsquo ; union try to work out a compromise . For most players who protest , it is probably a little of both . The protests had cooled off by last fall , until Trump ripped such players in a mildly profane speech , and they were a minor issue again by the end of last season , until the owners rewrote the anthem rules in May . Dying embers , gasoline , match , whoomp. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But , now that the fire has been rekindled , it&amp;rsquo ; s impossible to imagine dousing it again . Trump has made clear that he thinks every player on an NFL team must stand with &amp;ldquo ; hand on heart&amp;rdquo ; during the anthem . He finds the idea that any player would be allowed to remain in the locker room an outrage , a fact that surprised no one other than the league owners who thought the compromise they included in the new rules would placate those who were angered by the sight of a player on his knee . The President will continue to vent about the NFL until everyone does what he says they should . Over the course of this controversy , he&amp;rsquo ; s called for players to be fired over protests , and more recently said they should be suspended for a season without pay over a second kneeling offence . He&amp;rsquo ; s not exactly softening on this particular issue . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But the players aren&amp;rsquo ; t about to agree to a full-scale retreat , either . The league used to have no rules about the anthem , and then in 2009 it said players should be on the field for it , but there was no disciplinary measure for players who avoided the anthem , or kneeled , or sat on the bench or did cartwheels during it . This is why Kaepernick , despite becoming an unemployed pariah , was never punished while he was on a roster. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;5815;TOOLONG ... NFL owners : You kowtowed to Trump with anthem policy and only invited more **39;5850;TOOLONG &lt;p ... The league tried to change in the rules in May to allow players to be formally punished for kneeling , but now that the players pushed back they are seeking some kind of out . How would that look ? Are the players , now that they are at the table , going to agree to a set of rules that seeks to effectively end any type of protest during the anthem ? Because that&amp;rsquo ; s the only thing that will stop the periodic cries of SHAME from the White House . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is a uniquely NFL problem . The NBA , home of many a vocal Trump critic , requires players to stand for the anthem , and they are all fine with that . But their rule was in place many years ago , and they let players speak freely on social issues , and no one is calling for a basketball boycott . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But because it was the NFL where the anthem issue came to light , and the President made it a personal fight , it is the NFL that will continue to wear it . It has been suggested that the problem could be solved by eliminating the practice of playing the anthem before games . It&amp;rsquo ; s true that the anthem is unnecessary for a sporting event , but does anyone really think that scrapping The Star-Spangled Banner would cool things off ? I mean , imagine the tweets. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Instead , we wait to see if the league and the players can come up with a policy that they both like . It is hard to imagine a set of rules that will placate , say , Jerry Jones AND Malcolm Jenkins . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The only certain thing here , as the issue continues to unfold , is that Colin Kaepernick still won&amp;rsquo ; t have a job . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;em&gt;Email : &lt;a href= " **29;5891;TOOLONG " **36;5922;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **26;5960;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " **38;5988;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Scott StinsonFri , 17 Aug 2018 16:42:21 **45;6028;TOOLONG ... team and player all have something to gain or lose with Johnny Manziel experimenthttp : **30;6075;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;6107;TOOLONG ... title= " Quarterback Johnny Manziel , left , goes through drills with Hamilton Tiger-Cats teammate Jeremiah Masoli on the field at McMaster University on Sunday , May 20 , 2018.
