The first leg of each matchup is scheduled for mid-February , with the second legs to be played in March . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;27067;TOOLONG ... title= " In this Oct. 13 , 1984 file photo , Alan Trammell hits a two-run home run in the World Series for the Detroit Tigers . " alt= " In this Oct. 13 , 1984 file photo , Alan Trammell hits a two-run home run in the World Series for the Detroit Tigers . " data-portal-copyright= " Rusty Kennedy " **28;27102;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 77020047 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Detroit Tigers fans will have something to look forward to in 2018 after Alan Trammell and Jack Morris , members of the 1984 World Series-winning club , were elected to the Baseball Hall of Fame on Sunday by the Modern Era panel that was considering the candidacy of nine nominees whose careers started between 1970-87 . Trammell and Morris each received more than the 75 per cent of the votes needed among the 16 panel members . Former players association executive director Marvin Miller was also under consideration , but fell short by five votes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With their team contemplating a deep rebuild , Tigers fans will be able to mark induction day , July 29 , on the calendar . The ceremony will also include any players selected by Baseball Writers Association of America voters . Those results will be announced Jan. 24. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; More than 48 hours after it was first rumoured , the New York Yankees and Miami Marlins have finally completed their trade of 2017 NL MVP Giancarlo Stanton . AP reports the Marlins will receive second baseman Starlin Castro and prospects Jorge Guzman , a pitcher , and Jose Devers , an infielder . Guzman is now the No. 3 prospect in the Marlins system as rated by MLBPipeline.com . The Marlins will also send New York US$30 million to offset the $295 million remaining on Stanton&amp;rsquo ; s contract , provided he does not opt out of the contract when that window opens after the 2020 season . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;27132;TOOLONG ... title= " The New York Yankees acquired reigning NL MVP Giancarlo Stanton from the Miami Marlins on Dec. 11. " alt= " The New York Yankees acquired reigning NL MVP Giancarlo Stanton from the Miami Marlins on Dec. 11. " data-portal-copyright= " Eric Espada " **28;27167;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 77020004 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Nutritional analysis&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; Baseball&amp;rsquo ; s Winter Meetings kicked off Sunday in Orlando , which means the off-season is ready to begin in earnest . General managers and agents will be in one place and the movement of free agents can now start to shape rosters for the 2018 season . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Anecdotally , it seemed like the off-season was off to a slow start . As fans have become more invested in team-building machinations , the off-season has gained a brighter spotlight and it seemed like there was a dearth of movement once the World Series ended , qualifying offers were sorted out and teams knew which players were on the market . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sunday started the beginning of week 50 on the 2017 calendar . From the start of week 45 on Nov. 5 , there were only five major-league free-agent signings , the first of which didn&amp;rsquo ; t come until Nov. 20 . Only two of those five players had any sort of name recognition : the White Sox added catcher Welington Castillo , whom Jays fans remember from his five homers against Toronto in 2017 , and the Rangers signed pitcher Doug Fister , who saw duty with the Red Sox last season . Two of the other three were career minor-leaguers signed to major-league deals . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Additionally , there were 11 players signed to minor-league deals with invitations to the big clubs&amp;rsquo ; spring training camp , and 22 players were signed to straight minor-league contracts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Was this level of action out of the ordinary ? Let&amp;rsquo ; s check the data . The transaction data file at Retrosheet.org contains more than 88,000 moves made between December 1880 and February 2017 . True free agency didn&amp;rsquo ; t really become a factor in the majors until the reserve clause was bargained out of existence after the 1975 season following a court challenge by Curt Flood ( 1970 ) and an arbitration ruling against baseball in the cases of Andy Messersmith and Dave McNally ( 1975 ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Below is a chart showing the number of major-league free-agent signings by calendar week from 1976 to the opening of spring training in 2017. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;27197;TOOLONG ... class= " aligncenter size-full wp-image-77018310 " src= " https : **33;27232;TOOLONG ... alt= " " width= " 640 " height= " 485 " srcset= " https : **31;27267;TOOLONG ... 640w , https : **39;27300;TOOLONG ... 150w , https : **39;27341;TOOLONG ... 300w " sizes= " ( max-width : 640px ) 100vw , 640px " &gt;&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The data shows the peak of the free-agent season comes in week 51 after a sharp build in weeks 49 and 50 . The longer increase from the end of the post-season generally begins as early as week 43 , hitting a lower peak in week 46 and 47 before building again to the prime weeks . However , even though baseball is operating under the same basic rules since 1976 , that doesn&amp;rsquo ; t mean the current environment bears any similarity to the past . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Let&amp;rsquo ; s break it down to the last 10 seasons and see what the free-agent data shows . Below is a chart showing each week of free-agent signings from the end of the 2007 post-season to the beginning of 2017 spring training . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;27382;TOOLONG ... class= " aligncenter size-full wp-image-77018311 " src= " https : **33;27417;TOOLONG ... alt= " " width= " 640 " height= " 500 " srcset= " https : **31;27452;TOOLONG ... 640w , https : **39;27485;TOOLONG ... 150w , https : **39;27526;TOOLONG ... 300w " sizes= " ( max-width : 640px ) 100vw , 640px " &gt;&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; We see the same general shape of data with signings starting to ramp up in week 45 and the peak coming in weeks 50 and 51 . And if we zoom in on the last weeks of the year and look at the actual numbers , we can see that 2015 and 2016 have had their moves start to concentrate around week 50. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;27567;TOOLONG ... class= " aligncenter size-full wp-image-77018309 " src= " https : **33;27602;TOOLONG ... alt= " " width= " 640 " height= " 485 " srcset= " https : **31;27637;TOOLONG ... 640w , https : **39;27670;TOOLONG ... 150w , https : **39;27711;TOOLONG ... 300w " sizes= " ( max-width : 640px ) 100vw , 640px " &gt;&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What&amp;rsquo ; s unclear from the data is whether it&amp;rsquo ; s teams or players that are driving the delay to the free-agent season . We can say that this off-season has had some unusual circumstances that may have had an impact : several teams were using resources on the chase for Shohei Ohtani ; the availability of Stanton likely delayed some decision-making on the outfield and power-hitter free agents ; and the sudden availability of the 13 international free agents stripped from the Atlanta Braves also may have drawn attention from the major-league market . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But now that Ohtani , Stanton and Kevin Maitan , the top player from the Braves investigation , have landed in their new spots , and now that the Winter Meetings are on , we ought to see a significant number of signings over the next two weeks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Photo of the day&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; We swear there was a football game played in Buffalo yesterday afternoon . You just have to squint to see the evidence . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;At **27;27752;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; In case you missed it over the weekend , Scott Stinson was at BMO Field for Toronto FC&amp;rsquo ; s &lt;a href= " http : **34;27781;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;domineering , breakthrough victory&lt;/a&gt; over Seattle in the MLS Cup final . As Reds captain Michael Bradley said after the match , hoisting the league&amp;rsquo ; s playoff trophy &amp;ldquo ; has been an obsession , &amp;rdquo ; one that Sounders keeper Stefan Frei did his damnedest to try to ward off for a second straight year . Frei was incredible , Stinson writes , but on Saturday , TFC was even better . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; Seattleites can take solace in another bit of recent news : the NHL&amp;rsquo ; s revelation last week that it intends to expand to 32 teams &amp;mdash ; and that their city is first in line to apply . Will cool , rainy Seattle embrace &lt;a href= " http : **34;27817;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;the fastest game on ice&lt;/a&gt; ? Pontiac Silverdome proves to be tough to demolish in failed implosionhttp : **31;27955;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;27988;TOOLONG ... title= " Detonations can be seen during an attempted implosion of the Silverdome , in Pontiac , Michigan on Dec. 3 , 2017 . Technical problems with the explosives left the stadium still standing after the blast . " alt= " Detonations can be seen during an attempted implosion of the Silverdome , in Pontiac , Michigan on Dec. 3 , 2017 . " data-portal-copyright= " David Guralnick " **28;28023;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 77002324 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; PONTIAC , Mich. &amp;mdash ; A partial implosion of the Silverdome failed to bring down the upper section of the Detroit Lions&amp;rsquo ; former home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Demolition company Adamo says that Sunday morning&amp;rsquo ; s blasts did weaken the Silverdome and it could still fall , but it&amp;rsquo ; s unclear when that might happen . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;28053;TOOLONG ... title= " Detonations can be seen during an attempted implosion of the Silverdome , in Pontiac , Michigan on Dec. 3 , 2017 . Technical problems with the explosives left the stadium still standing after the blast . " alt= " Detonations can be seen during an attempted implosion of the Silverdome , in Pontiac , Michigan on Dec. 3 , 2017 . Technical problems with the explosives left the stadium still standing after the blast . " data-portal-copyright= " David Guralnick " **28;28088;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 77002324 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rick Cuppetilli , executive vice-president with Adamo , told the Detroit Free Press that 10 per cent of the explosive charges failed to detonate because of wiring issues that crews are investigating . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Cuppetilli says that unless gravity causes the building to fall on its own , excavators will start taking down the structure this week . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;28118;TOOLONG ... title= " Steel , broken from the explosions , hang from the roof after an attempted implosion of the Silverdome. " alt= " Steel , broken from the explosions , hang from the roof after an attempted implosion of the Silverdome. " data-portal-copyright= " David Guralnick " **28;28153;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 77002327 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Silverdome was opened by the city of Pontiac in 1975 and the Lions left Tiger Stadium to play there . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Lions played in Detroit&amp;rsquo ; s northern suburb from 1975 through 2001 . At 80,300 seats for football , it was the largest facility in the league until 1997 when Washington&amp;rsquo ; s FedEx Field eclipsed it . The Silverdome also was the home of the NBA&amp;rsquo ; s Detroit Pistons from 1978-88 and the Michigan Panthers of the USFL played their two seasons there , winning the first league title in 1983. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was the site of Super Bowl XVI between the San Francisco 49ers and Cincinnati Bengals in 1982 . In 1994 , it staged four group-stage matches in soccer&amp;rsquo ; s World Cup . The stadium drew more than 90,000 spectators for Wrestlemania III in March 1987 and for a mass by Pope John Paul II in September 1987. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The stadium was purchased at auction in 2009 by Toronto-based Triple Properties Inc . A handful of events were staged there but the stadium never found a permanent replacement tenant . Redevelopment plans never materialized . Eventually the air pressure that supported the fiberglass roof was released and the roof slowly collapsed in 2013 . The panels then tore from wind and snow damage and the stadium became a blight on the landscape . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Oakland Press &lt;a href= " http : **34;28183;TOOLONG ... rel= " noopener " target= " blank " &gt;reported&lt;/a&gt; in October that the 127-acre site is being offered for sale for about $30 million to real estate developers who would repurpose the area . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The video below offers better sound of the blasts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;span class= " embed-youtube " style= " text-align:center ; display : block ; " &gt;&lt;iframe class='youtube-player ' type='text/html ' width= ' 640 ' height= ' 390 ' src='https : **34;28219;TOOLONG ... allowfullscreen='true ' style='border:0 ; ' **28;28255;TOOLONG ... Post Wire ServicesSun , 03 Dec 2017 19:13:35 **45;28285;TOOLONG ... your popcorn and watch Jerry Jones try to bring the NFL and Roger Goodell to heelhttp : **36;28332;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;28370;TOOLONG ... title= " Dallas Cowboys owner Jerry Jones , left , stands on the field prior to his team 's game against the San Francisco 49ers at Levi 's Stadium on October 22 , 2017. " alt= " Dallas Cowboys owner Jerry Jones , left , stands on the field prior to his team 's game against the San Francisco 49ers at Levi 's Stadium on October 22 , 2017. " data-portal-copyright= " Ezra Shaw " **28;28405;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 76966544 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was a lot simpler when you could just sit back and judge Jerry Jones for his avarice and his hubris . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jones , the Dallas Cowboys&amp;rsquo ; owner , is a model villain . He built a gaudy football stadium , helped by more than US$300 million from local government , and last year opened an even-more-gaudy practice facility , complete with luxury hotel , the anticipated billion-dollar cost of which was , again , supported by funds from the local municipality and the school ( ! ) district . His ego led him to boot the exceedingly successful Jimmy Johnson from the team , and at 75 years old he continues to run the Cowboys&amp;rsquo ; football operations despite not getting to a conference finals since 1995 . He&amp;rsquo ; s downplayed football&amp;rsquo ; s brain-injury problem , sued the NFL when he didn&amp;rsquo ; t get his way on licensing deals and , notably , publicly supported the league&amp;rsquo ; s bumbling commissioner when almost no one else would . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And now it is hard not to root for him . For months Jones has been impeding the contract extension for that same commissioner , which was thought to be mostly a formality as recently as August . Roger Goodell&amp;rsquo ; s contract runs through 2019 and pays him an absurd $42 million annually , and in the late summer the league&amp;rsquo ; s owners voted &amp;mdash ; unanimously &amp;mdash ; to let a small committee work out the details of the next contract . Jones , though , flipped on Goodell , and is now said to be determined to make him take much less guaranteed money , and has threatened to sue ( again ) if his fellow owners do not agree . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This has been explained in some quarters as Jones belatedly coming to the realization that 40-something-million for a commissioner who has never handled a player-discipline matter that he couldn&amp;rsquo ; t spectacularly botch , and who is presiding over a league that is facing many serious long-term challenges , is rather too much money . But it is also plainly evident that Jones changed his mind on Goodell right about the time that the commissioner decided to suspend the Cowboys&amp;rsquo ; star running back , Ezekiel Elliott , for six games for violating the league&amp;rsquo ; s domestic-violence policy . ESPN published a report on Friday , authored by Seth Wickersham and Don Van Natta , Jr. , who holds a master&amp;rsquo ; s degree in Jerryology , that said Jones was furious about the Elliott suspension , that Goodell had assured him that the allegations of physical abuse from a former girlfriend of Elliott lacked evidence , and that Jones considered the harsh sentence against one of his best players a complete betrayal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;28435;TOOLONG ... title= " NFL commissioner Roger Goodell , left and Dallas Cowboys owner Jerry Jones in London on Nov. 9 , 2014. " alt= " NFL commissioner Roger Goodell , left and Dallas Cowboys owner Jerry Jones in London on Nov. 9 , 2014. " data-portal-copyright= " Matt Dunham " **28;28470;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 76966545 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; And while one can certainly question the purity of Jones&amp;rsquo ; motivation &amp;mdash ; he didn&amp;rsquo ; t seem to complain much when Goodell suspended Tom Brady after one of his famously torqued investigations &amp;mdash ; it&amp;rsquo ; s also true that the man has a point : Goodell is awful at this . The Elliott case was supposed to be the test run of the NFL&amp;rsquo ; s new domestic-violence policy , which was developed after Goodell embarrassed himself and the league in the Ray Rice case . Instead of the half-assed investigation that blew up in Goodell&amp;rsquo ; s face when video evidence emerged against Rice after a light punishment , the new policy would see league investigators spend much time and money before handing the results over to Goodell for consideration . One could wonder whether sports leagues should be in the quasi-judicial investigation business at all , but at least the NFL could no longer be accused of not taking allegations against its players seriously . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And then Goodell botched the Elliott case anyway . After a year-long investigation in which the NFL&amp;rsquo ; s own lead investigator found the accuser not to be a credible witness and recommended against a suspension , Goodell ruled in the opposite direction . Rather than offer transparency , the league kept its investigators&amp;rsquo ; concerns out of the ruling , instead issuing a letter with many damning details about what Elliott is alleged to have done to the former girlfriend in the spring of 2016 . While only Elliott and his alleged victim know what truly happened , Goodell&amp;rsquo ; s handling of the case looks entirely like someone who , faced with difficult and contradictory evidence , decided he had to pick a side and then disregarded everything that might have supported the other side . It might seem unfair to the player , and through two months of appeals several courts have agreed , but this is ultimately a system in which the standard of proof is whatever the commissioner wants it to be . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It bears repeating that Jones was fine with all the other stuff that Goodell has overseen as commissioner : the foot-dragging on concussions , the shameless and dangerous move to weekly Thursday night games , the franchise relocations , the falling television ratings . Jones even played a lead role in some of that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But if the Elliott matter , and his obvious bias in it , is the thing that finally turned Jones against Goodell , I am fine with that . Friday&amp;rsquo ; s ESPN report includes a money quote in which Jones is said to have told Goodell that he should want no part of the Wrath of Jerry . Invoking Patriots owner Robert Kraft , once displeased with the Brady suspension , Jones said : &amp;ldquo ; Bob Kraft is a p&amp;ndash ; &amp;ndash ; &amp;ndash ; y compared to what I&amp;rsquo ; m going to do . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; I don&amp;rsquo ; t think he said &amp;ldquo ; party . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As that comment shows , Jones could easily alienate as many fellow owners as he brings to his side in his fight with Goodell . The prospect of a great big squabble among the NFL&amp;rsquo ; s high-and-mighty billionaires club ? I am fine with that , too . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; sstinson@postmedia.com &lt;/p&gt; Scott StinsonFri , 17 Nov 2017 17:54:25 **45;28500;TOOLONG ... quarterback Aaron Rodgers has broken collarbone , season in doubthttp : **35;28547;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;28584;TOOLONG ... title= " Green Bay Packers quarterback Aaron Rodgers is attended to by medical staff after being hit by Minnesota Vikings linebacker Anthony Barr in the first half of Sunday 's game in Minneapolis . " data-portal-copyright= " Bruce Kluckhohn " **28;28619;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 76874141 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; MINNEAPOLIS &amp;mdash ; Green Bay Packers star quarterback Aaron Rodgers could miss the remainder of the season with a broken collarbone. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rodgers left the game against Minnesota in the first quarter on Sunday , and the Packers announced at halftime that his season is in jeopardy after the injury was diagnosed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There is a chance he could miss the remainder of the season , &amp;rdquo ; the Packers tweeted. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;blockquote class= " twitter-tweet " data-width= " 550 " &gt;&lt;p lang= " en " dir= " ltr " &gt;Aaron Rodgers hit and knocked out on this play &lt;a href= " https : //t.co/PDr0kxgIW6 " &gt;pic.twitter ... -- Vikings Blogger ( @firstandskol ) &lt;a href= " https : **33;28649;TOOLONG ... 15 , **33;28684;TOOLONG &lt;script async src= " **33;28719;TOOLONG " charset= " utf-8 " &gt;&lt;/script&gt; &lt;p&gt; Minnesota won 23-10 to move into a tie with Green Bay for the NFC North Division lead at 4-2. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s a crushing blow to a team that entered the season with Super Bowl aspirations , optimism based primarily on the right arm of Rodgers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Despite several key injuries to the secondary , the Packers entered the game Sunday against their NFC North rivals at 4-1 after Rodgers engineered a game-winning touchdown drive to beat the Dallas Cowboys last week . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Vikings coach Mike Zimmer&amp;rsquo ; s defence has always gotten after Rodgers in these tense matchups , and it happened again in the first quarter when linebacker Anthony Barr drove him into the turf after he got rid of a roll-out pass . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The impact did not appear to be that violent at first glance as Rodgers rolled through the hit . But it became apparent quickly that he was in considerable pain . He writhed on the turf as Packers coach Mike McCarthy argued for a roughing the passer penalty . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As Rodgers walked gingerly to the sideline , he turned to jaw at Barr before entering a tent on the sideline for examination . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eventually he rode a cart to the locker room for further tests and remained there throughout the first half . He was initially deemed questionable , but the Packers announced after halftime that he would not return to the game . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He was 2 of 4 for 18 yards before leaving , casting a pall over the rivalry that always includes thousands of Packers fans in Minnesota&amp;rsquo ; s stadium . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Brett Hundley , the only other quarterback on the Packers roster , stepped in for Rodgers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A Packers offence without Rodgers under centre is not something Green Bay has seen very often . Hundley entered the game having thrown 11 career passes , all coming in the fourth quarters of mop-up duty . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Since becoming the full-time starter in 2008 , Rodgers has been remarkably durable . He missed seven games in 2013 with a fractured collarbone , but he has started 132 of the other 134 games during that time period . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Of course , being able to count on a quarterback to take the snaps is nothing new for Green Bay . Since Brett Favre took over for the injured Don Majkowski in the third game of the 1992 season , the Packers have had Favre and Rodgers for all but nine games . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Matt Flynn , Scott Tolzien and Seneca Wallace were forced to fill in while Rodgers was out in 2013 . Flynn started once in 2010 when Rodgers sat out with a concussion and again in 2011 when he rested during the regular-season finale with the Packers having clinched the top seed in the NFC playoffs. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Favre never sat at all . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I hope @AaronRodgers12 is OK ! &amp;rdquo ; Seahawks cornerback Richard Sherman tweeted. &amp;ldquo ; The league is better when he&amp;rsquo ; s healthy . Praying for you . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hundley&amp;rsquo ; s first pass of the game was intercepted by Xavier Rhodes , but he recovered later in the second quarter and was able to step up in the pocket and hit a wide open Davante Adams for a touchdown that tied the game at 7. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hundley was a fifth-round draft pick by the Packers in 2015 . He was a three-year starter at UCLA . He had only thrown 11 passes in the NFL before being thrown into the fire on Sunday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Rodgers broke his left collarbone in November 2013 and he missed seven games , returning for the final game of the regular season . Green Bay was 0-4-1 in the first five games without Rodgers but rebounded to win two of the last three with Flynn under centre before Rodgers returned . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Packers , who finished 8-7-1 , lost to San Francisco in the first round of the playoffs. &lt;/p&gt; The Associated PressSun , 15 Oct 2017 20:52:50 **45;28754;TOOLONG ... owners decide to concede the sack against Trump 's anthem blitzhttp : **35;28801;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;28838;TOOLONG ... title= " Defensive tackle Damon Harrison and strong safety Landon Collins of the New York Giants lock arms and raise their right fists into the air during the playing of the national anthem before the start of an NFL football game in Tampa , Florida on Oct. 1 . 2017. " data-portal-copyright= " Brian Blanco " **28;28873;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 76866903 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The least surprising development in Donald Trump&amp;rsquo ; s War on Football is that the unfettered support that NFL owners appeared to show for their players in the early days has turned out to be rather more fettered . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is a league , after all , in which the players are at extraordinary risk of sudden career-ending injury , and at a significant risk of long-term post-career injury , and yet their contracts are not fully guaranteed . Luke Kuechly , the Carolina linebacker , suffered his third concussion in two years on Thursday night , and , at 26 years old , his future has to at least be in some doubt . The Panthers can simply cut him someday and save themselves some or all of the $27.4 million that isn&amp;rsquo ; t guaranteed on the five-year extension that just began this season . Tough luck , son . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This is a league in which players have had to bargain and fight for every incremental safety measure &amp;mdash ; fewer full-contact practices , a lighter off-season workload &amp;mdash ; only to see the league turn around and make Thursday Night Football and its insane three-day recovery period after three hours of simulated car accidents a weekly thing . This is a league that has been sued by former players about concussions and about painkiller abuse , and if you take the time to read either of those class-actions you get the picture of teams who treat their athletes as cannon fodder . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Did we really expect that when the NFL&amp;rsquo ; s billionaire owners were stuck with a decision between supporting their players and worrying about their revenues , they would choose the former ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But , after early rebukes from owners of the U.S. president&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; divisive&amp;rdquo ; and &amp;ldquo ; inappropriate&amp;rdquo ; language &amp;mdash ; references to his &amp;ldquo ; son of a bitch&amp;rdquo ; comments in a speech in Alabama three weeks ago &amp;mdash ; and implicit approval of the players&amp;rsquo ; right to kneel during the national anthem , that turned into choreographed displays of non-kneeling unity and , now , some explicit bans on kneeling during the anthem from certain owners . The league itself vows to put the anthem controversy &amp;ldquo ; front and centre&amp;rdquo ; during a meeting of league owners next week , and while it has been careful to clarify that it has not yet adopted Trump&amp;rsquo ; s **40;28903;TOOLONG edict , it is plainly obvious that the NFL would like to convince players to stop kneeling so everyone can get back to cheering them on as they violently hurl their bodies into each other without fear of having the occasion tainted by a brief , silent social protest . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s a win for Trump , so far , who took what was originally a protest about racial inequality and police brutality and made it about patriotism . He convinced his supporters that the playing of the national anthem at sporting events was an utterly sombre moment of devotion to one&amp;rsquo ; s country and the military , even though the stadiums are full of people buying beer and nachos and using the toilet while The Star-Spangled Banner is sung . Stephen Ross , the owner of the Miami Dolphins and someone who supported players who knelt even last season , when few were doing it , said last week that they had effectively been checkmated by the president . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; ( Trump ) has changed that whole paradigm of what protest is . I think it&amp;rsquo ; s incumbent upon the players today , because of how the public is looking at it , is to stand and salute the flag , &amp;rdquo ; he told a Miami newspaper . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Leaving aside the problem with that explanation &amp;mdash ; what the players were doing was right , but Trump says it is something else so now it is not right &amp;mdash ; what Ross and like-minded owners are essentially saying is that they will concede the president&amp;rsquo ; s point if he gets enough people worked up about it . The New York Times published a poll this week that showed Trump voters had suddenly developed negative views about the NFL , right when the president started complaining about players and the reluctance of the league to punish them . The results were so striking that it suggests that if Trump decides tomorrow that he&amp;rsquo ; s going to tweet angrily about Coke , the smart move is to load up on Pepsi stock . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But if the owners and the NFL believe that all will be solved if they can just get players to stop making their statements during the now-hallowed anthem , I would bet many athletes will not see this as the end of it . What if someone takes a knee after sacking the quarterback ? Or raises a fist after scoring a touchdown ? After all , Trump has said the problem is players disrespecting the flag , not their right to protest . Would he still blast them if they showed defiance in some other way ? ( Spoiler : Yes. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trump undoubtedly has the owners and the commissioner playing defence , and yet there have to be many players who are not anxious to concede defeat . It&amp;rsquo ; s not just that they have received support from those who encourage them to speak out about things like inequality , but they are aware of the guy at the centre of the controversy , too . " data-portal-copyright= " Veronica Henri " **28;29845;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 76691063 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; TORONTO &amp;mdash ; Masai Ujiri was speaking for the Toronto Raptors on Monday , but he might as well have been speaking for the entire sports world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I can guarantee you one thing : nobody&amp;rsquo ; s getting fired here , &amp;rdquo ; Ujiri , the Raptors&amp;rsquo ; president , said on back-to-school day for the basketball team , the opening of training camp , which happened to coincide with one of the strangest sports weekends in memory . &amp;ldquo ; ( The players ) have a platform , &amp;rdquo ; Ujiri said . &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s nobody getting fired . Sports franchises , almost without exception , preach about the importance of limiting distractions , which is code for not saying anything controversial . In practice , this tends to mean players and coaches avoid taking a position on issues of the day , even when it falls in their lap . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;29875;TOOLONG ... title= " Cleveland Cavaliers forward LeBron James speaks to reporters at his team 's media day on Sept. 25. New England quarterback Tom Brady , a noted Trump supporter , smiled and said he was willing to talk football . His coach , Bill Belichick , did not smile and said the same . " data-portal-copyright= " Chris Young " **28;29975;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 76691074 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; He&amp;rsquo ; s correct that there is a stay-in-your-lane element to much of the criticism of the athletes , even if they are just silently kneeling . " data-portal-copyright= " Frank Franklin II " **28;30258;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 76679102 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; If you drew a Venn diagram of those who felt that Colin Kaepernick should have kept his politics separate from his NFL job , and those who applauded Donald Trump for calling players who kneel during the national anthem &amp;ldquo ; sons of bitches&amp;rdquo ; who deserve to be fired , I suspect it would look a lot like a single circle . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That sort of cognitive dissonance has become a staple of our times . So , much groaning and fist-shaking when an athlete dares not stick to sports , but also furious nodding and fist-pumping when the U.S. President spends his weekend blasting football players , disinviting an NBA team to the White House and then calling for an NFL boycott when it becomes clear that the league is not about to go on a firing spree at his behest . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That Trump has found a receptive audience for his ranting is not a surprise . ( That&amp;rsquo ; s another staple of our times : there is always an audience for his rantings . ) But that the NFL has pushed back so significantly is at least somewhat unexpected . This is a league run by exceedingly wealthy men , several of whom proudly call Trump a friend and who donated millions to his political cause . In this one way , the most divisive president in history has finally managed to be a uniter. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Before Sunday , the number of people around the NFL who had shown much public support for the Kaepernick cause was tiny . You could have barely fielded a starting 11 , let alone filled out a 53-man roster with those who felt strongly enough about drawing attention to racial injustice to join Kaepernick in kneeling during The Star-Spangled Banner . Kaepernick himself was out of a job , and though a few players said they thought he should be on a roster , the season began and he most obviously was not . Other than the few players showing solidarity with him , that was that . The story had run its course . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;30288;TOOLONG ... title= " San Francisco 49ers safety Eric Reid ( left ) and quarterback Colin Kaepernick kneel during the national anthem before a game against the Los Angeles Rams on Sept. 12 , 2016. " data-portal-copyright= " Marcio Jose Sanchez " **28;30323;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 1207452 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trump promptly took those dying embers , dumped gasoline on them , and fired up his blowtorch . It&amp;rsquo ; s not that every team is suddenly populated with dozens of players who cared enough about police brutality or racial inequality to kneel on the sidelines , but the league , almost as a whole , took issue with the president&amp;rsquo ; s assertion that players should be fired over an act of peaceful protest . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Owners , executives , broadcasters , coaches , players : Sunday was a mobilization of people talking politics when normally they would much rather be discussing imposing their will and establishing the run and various other cliches . Set aside whatever you might think about kneeling during an anthem , or raising one&amp;rsquo ; s fist : the backlash isn&amp;rsquo ; t about that . It&amp;rsquo ; s about the right to do it , the right of an NFL player to make a benign statement without the country&amp;rsquo ; s elected leader calling on them to lose their job . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When the ESPN anchor Jemele Hill called Trump a white supremacist on Twitter recently and the White House called for her to be fired , the president&amp;rsquo ; s defenders said his position was justified because what Hill had said was so inflammatory . But in his war with the NFL &amp;mdash ; one entirely of his making , since the comment that started it all came unprompted in a Friday night speech &amp;mdash ; Trump has made clear that you don&amp;rsquo ; t even have to saying anything particularly untoward to be worthy of dismissal from your job . You don&amp;rsquo ; t even have to say anything at all . You just have to do something that he doesn&amp;rsquo ; t like . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trump will no doubt continue to insist that players should be fired for kneeling , and they will keep doing it , acutely aware that there is now much safety in numbers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;30353;TOOLONG ... title= " U.S. President Donald Trump seemed to suddenly fixate on the issue of players kneeling during the national anthem during a rally in Alabama on Friday night , in which he suggested that coaches should get the " son of a bitch " players off the field if they continued to kneel . " alt= " U.S. President Donald Trump seemed to suddenly fixate on the issue of players kneeling during the national anthem during a rally in Alabama on Friday night , in which he suggested that coaches should get the " son of a bitch " players off the field if they continued to kneel . " data-portal-copyright= " Brendan Smialowski " **28;30388;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 76674830 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the NBA , where big names like Gregg Popovich and Steve Kerr were already firmly anti-Trump , the fact that the Golden State Warriors have been told they are not welcome at the White House will be a point of pride . It would be a surprise if the NBA season didn&amp;rsquo ; t open next month with players making a show of protest during the anthem ceremonies . Bruce Maxwell of the Oakland A&amp;rsquo ; s kneeled during the anthem on Saturday night , and other baseball players may yet follow . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The NHL , which has been largely immune to the various Trump controversies due at least in part to the relative lack of Americans &amp;mdash ; and almost total lack of African-Americans &amp;mdash ; playing in it , managed to insert itself right in the middle of the discussion , and not in a good way , on Sunday when the Pittsburgh Penguins announced that they would visit the White House , as champions do . Good timing there , fellas . Pittsburgh Penguins issue statement : &amp;ldquo ; Our league is extremely white . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But it&amp;rsquo ; s with the NFL where this story will reside , for now . For all of its flaws , it&amp;rsquo ; s America&amp;rsquo ; s most popular sports league , and the President decided to pick a fight with it . Did he think that an organization full of some of the largest humans on the planet would respond meekly to being bullied ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Instead , he spurred dozens of players throughout the league to openly defy his toothless commands , and hundreds more to lock arms with one another in a show of unity . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Next time , Trump might want to stick to firing his own employees . He&amp;rsquo ; s quite good at that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;em&gt;Email : &lt;a href= " **29;30418;TOOLONG " **36;30449;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a class= " twitter-follow-button " href= " https : **26;30487;TOOLONG " **38;30515;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Scott StinsonSun , 24 Sep 2017 20:08:55 **45;30555;TOOLONG ... does the CTE diagnosis of murderer Aaron Hernandez fit into the large concussion context ? http : **32;30602;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;30636;TOOLONG ... title= " Former New England Patriots tight end Aaron Hernandez listens to a pretrial hearing in Boston in this Dec. 27 , 2016 file photo . " alt= " Former New England Patriots tight end Aaron Hernandez listens to a pretrial hearing in Boston in this Dec. 27 , 2016 file photo . " data-portal-copyright= " Josh Reynolds " **28;30671;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 76657841 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Aaron Hernandez story is very much one of extremes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A national champion at the University of Florida and then part of a historically good tight-end duo with the New England Patriots , Hernandez would set a new standard for &amp;ldquo ; off-field issues&amp;rdquo ; when he was convicted of murdering a friend &amp;mdash ; a clumsy , dumb and unfathomable crime that took place after his third season in the NFL , when he was a wealthy star on the rise . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And now his brain has been discovered to show evidence of chronic traumatic encephalopathy ( CTE ) , the brain-wasting disease prevalent in so many former football players . Hernandez&amp;rsquo ; s lawyer said researchers told him it was the most severe case of CTE they had seen in someone his age . He was 27 when he hung himself in prison , not long after his murder conviction . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But if the specifics of the Hernandez case are unusual , they are only so by a matter of degrees . His arc &amp;mdash ; football player dies , has brain examined , is found to have signs of CTE &amp;mdash ; is utterly familiar . Many families of former players have observed behavioural changes , mental suffering that can lead to suicide , which are then explained , somewhat , by the CTE that is discovered post-mortem . Hernandez&amp;rsquo ; s lawyers will now attempt to do something unique , which is blame murderous behaviour on the damage caused by football , but it&amp;rsquo ; s of a piece with the blame placed on the sport by the families of dozens of former players who only ended up harming themselves . Hernandez , that is , follows a well-established pattern , even if his story is particularly chilling . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And yet , his experience is also a confounding one . He was finished football at the age of 23 , having played three seasons and 38 games with New England . ( He also played in 40 games at the University of Florida . ) A pass-catching tight end , he was not the kind of player who spent long stretches bashing into opponents in the sport&amp;rsquo ; s trenches . There is no doubt that he would have taken blows to the head in his playing days , but because his career ended so early , Hernandez had simply not played that much football . When the first discoveries of CTE in former football players were made , after the alarming post-career struggles of players such as offensive lineman Mike Webster and linebacker Junior Seau , their brain damage was attributed to the years of repeated hits to their heads . Webster played in 245 NFL games over 16 years , Seau in 268 games over almost 20 years . That&amp;rsquo ; s seven times as many games as Hernandez , and countless more hits . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;30701;TOOLONG ... title= " Former New England Patriots tight end Aaron Hernandez listens to a pretrial hearing in Boston in this Dec. 27 , 2016 file photo . " alt= " Former New England Patriots tight end Aaron Hernandez listens to a pretrial hearing in Boston in this Dec. 27 , 2016 file photo . " data-portal-copyright= " Josh Reynolds " **28;30736;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 76657841 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; What this means for the continuing study of football and CTE is unclear . The Hernandez case has quickly been interpreted by some as evidence that the proper amount of football that can be safely played is none at all . If someone at a relatively young age who has played relatively few games can have &amp;ldquo ; severe&amp;rdquo ; CTE , how could anyone be confident that they could play the game and avoid brain trauma ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But there&amp;rsquo ; s another way to look at his case : Given his short career , is there another explanation for the damage to his brain other than football ? Was it there all along , or is there something in his physiology that made him particularly susceptible to developing CTE ? The great unknown with the disease , because it can presently only be discovered in the brains of the dead , is whether it also lurks in the brains of the non-football-playing public . Are there people who are at elevated risk of the disease , people for whom contact sports would have a dramatic impact on their brain&amp;rsquo ; s health in a short period of time ? Was Hernandez one of them ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There aren&amp;rsquo ; t going to be any satisfying answers to those questions , at least in this case . Hernandez&amp;rsquo ; s lawyers have an obvious financial incentive to blame the ruin of his promising life on the sport he played , but the 2013 murder of Odin Lloyd was cold and calculated . The courts will spend years sorting that out . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the meantime , there is the unavoidable fact that someone who didn&amp;rsquo ; t play that much football had the brain of someone who played for a dozen years . We say every time another study is released that shows a shocking prevalence of CTE in former football players that it adds to the growing understanding of the disease . We wonder when young men will start deciding in large numbers that the risk from the sport is too great . Hernandez , ever the outlier , probably won&amp;rsquo ; t change many minds . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But when someone with limited exposure to the game can still have such a damaged brain , it&amp;rsquo ; s hard not to wonder if the safety measures being enacted , the non-contact practices and the independent concussion spotters and the like , aren&amp;rsquo ; t just so much messing about in the margins . The pro leagues , when they aren&amp;rsquo ; t defending themselves in court , spend a lot of time these days discussing ways to make football safe . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Every passing season , though , stories like this one make it feel more like an impossible pursuit . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;bull ; &lt;em&gt;Email : &lt;a href= " **29;30766;TOOLONG " **36;30797;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **26;30835;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " **38;30863;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Scott StinsonFri , 22 Sep 2017 15:51:19 **45;30903;TOOLONG ... offers disgruntled NFL streaming subscribers a $20 refund for botched Canadian launchhttp : **34;30950;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;30986;TOOLONG ... title= " James Rushton , CEO of DAZN , at the company 's Canadian launch party in Toronto in August . " data-portal-copyright= " Bob Levey " **28;31167;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 76630438 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; These are troubled , divisive times , what with the missiles and the hoods and torches and the biblical storms . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So let us join together , then , in the Goodellenfreude. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Roger Goodell , as you have probably heard , is paid vast sums of money &amp;mdash ; more than US$40 million a year &amp;mdash ; as the NFL commissioner , even though he would have been outperformed in that role by a potted fern . Think of all the ferns the league&amp;rsquo ; s owners could get for $40 million . They would be able to select some truly choice house plants . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Goodell , as you might not have heard , was trundling along toward a contract extension , with the ubiquitous NFL insiders reporting that it was expected to be finalized soon , when it emerged that Jerry Jones was struck by the realization that perhaps the 32 league owners had not received a lot of value for money from Goodell. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This seems a little like just now realizing that footballs bounce funny , but it is worth celebrating nonetheless . Jones , the Dallas Cowboys owner , makes for a much better cartoon villain than he does a hero , but we will take it . The enemy of my enemy , etc. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jones , the owner of the biggest , wealthiest team in the NFL and thus one of its most powerful figures , is apparently aggrieved by Goodell&amp;rsquo ; s handling of the six-game suspension levied on Dallas running back Ezekiel Elliott for allegedly assaulting a then-girlfriend in the summer of 2016 , before his rookie season . As with seemingly every high-profile discipline case that the commissioner handles , it has blown up spectacularly in his face . A judge called the entire process &amp;ldquo ; fundamentally unfair&amp;rdquo ; to Elliott , granted the player an injunction , and it now looks like it will take until next season before any punishment is served , if at all . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;31197;TOOLONG ... title= " Dallas Cowboys running back Ezekiel Elliott warms up before an away game against the Denver Broncos on Sept. 17. " alt= " Dallas Cowboys running back Ezekiel Elliott warms up before an away game against the Denver Broncos on Sept. 17. " data-portal-copyright= " Justin Edmonds " **28;31232;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 76630440 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; That the commissioner has botched the case is unsurprising , given his track record , but what is unusual is that there was a brief period where it looked like he had handled this correctly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Acting in response to previous discipline embarrassments , this time the league conducted a lengthy investigation of its own &amp;mdash ; Elliott was never criminally charged &amp;mdash ; and determined that he caused injuries to the young woman and so would receive an automatic six-game ban , per league policy . That sounded reasonable enough : either a league should only discipline players who have been found guilty of a crime , or , if it is going to conduct its own investigation , it has to do it thoroughly . Somewhere in the middle , a half-assed cursory investigation and a light punishment ( hello , Ray Rice ) , is the worst of options . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The legal fighting has since revealed that Goodell ignored the advice of his own lead investigator and torqued the process against Elliott . To be clear , he may very well deserve a long suspension , but it looks like the league decided it wanted to come down hard and then presented only the evidence in support of that outcome . When you are developing your own quasi-judicial system , best not model it after the Soviets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This has been Goodell&amp;rsquo ; s pattern , to hoard all the power to impose justice , to both issue the sentence and then to hear the appeal &amp;mdash ; great sentence , judge ! &amp;mdash ; and then to watch as the case is torn to pieces . In recent years he has run afoul of Robert Kraft in New England and now Jones , and if you were drawing up a list of owners who could pull off a coup , those two would be near the top . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Goodell&amp;rsquo ; s survival over the years is usually attributed to two things : the vast money hose of the NFL&amp;rsquo ; s TV contracts , which keeps owners fat and happy , and the CBA with the players , which does the same thing . ( I would add that Goodell has also taken a lot of bullets on the concussion file , and the owners don&amp;rsquo ; t mind him drawing all that fire. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;31262;TOOLONG ... title= " In this March 11 , 2011 file photo , NFL commissioner Roger Goodell ( left ) and Dallas Cowboys owner Jerry Jones speak during the NFL lockout in Washington , D.C. " alt= " In this March 11 , 2011 file photo , NFL commissioner Roger Goodell ( left ) and Dallas Cowboys owner Jerry Jones speak during the NFL lockout in Washington , D.C. " data-portal-copyright= " Rob Carr " **28;31297;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 76630441 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The TV money would have come , though , regardless of who was in the commissioner&amp;rsquo ; s office , as indeed it has exploded across all sports in recent years . And on the CBA , signed in 2011 , it dramatically cut the amount spent on rookies , which meant teams could avoid the $50-million first-round bust . But that system has produced a league in which more teams use cheap first- and second-year players for salary-cap purposes , which goes some way to explaining why there is so much bad football every Sunday . ( And especially on Thursdays , another Goodell-era innovation that produces awful games while also completely exposing as hollow anything the commissioner might say about the importance of player safety. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Oh yes , and three teams have in recent months given the metaphorical finger to their fans and decided to move to new cities . Los Angeles now has two of those teams , and doesn&amp;rsquo ; t appear to want either of them . It is something to watch a huge city react with utter indifference to the Rams and Chargers , evidence that the mighty NFL , which could long do whatever it wanted , has now discovered that it can not just come to town and expect people to swoon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " http : **34;31327;TOOLONG ... not clear what the NFL thought it was getting itself into with **40;31363;TOOLONG href= " http : **34;31405;TOOLONG ... safest bet in sports : Men whining about a female announcer 's **37;31441;TOOLONG &lt;p &g; ... That arrogance is the hallmark of the Goodell years . In so many ways , the NFL says and does things without fear of impunity , because it assumes it will always win in the end . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s not a shock that Goodell might end up paying a price for such hubris . I&amp;rsquo ; m not complaining about the comparison . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Stampeders lead the CFL with a 10-1-1 record this season and are 25-3-2 over the last two regular seasons . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That record is nothing new for a team that is now 127-44-3 since Hufnagel joined the organization in 2008 . " data-portal-copyright= " Nathan Denette " **28;31868;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 76621140 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; It would be fair to say in the case of the Stampeders that winning breeds more winning . OUT A STAR&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; When the Stampeders used the seventh pick of the 2016 CFL draft to select linebacker Alex Singleton , they were pretty sure they were getting a steal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It turns out they were absolutely right , as Singleton has proven to be an elite talent and the best player from that draft class . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Singleton , an American with non-import status , set a CFL record by recording at least 10 tackles in each of the last three games . Predictably , he was named one of the league&amp;rsquo ; s top performers of the week on Tuesday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He has 91 defensive tackles through 12 games this season . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s not a surprise , truthfully , &amp;rdquo ; Hufnagel said . &amp;ldquo ; Again , this is good fortune . We had , I think , the seventh pick that year and he was still on the board . If we had the No. 1 pick in the draft , we would have selected him . We thought he was that good of a player and he has not disappointed us. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;TICAT SUSPENDED&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hamilton receiver Mike Jones was slapped with a two-game suspension , effective immediately , on Tuesday for violating the CFL&amp;rsquo ; s drug policy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jones apologized to the CFL , the CFLPA , the Tiger-Cats and his family in a statement after testing positive for an anabolic steroid called **30;32048;TOOLONG metabolite . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I had never heard of this substance and I have never intentionally consumed it , &amp;rdquo ; Jones said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He and the Tiger-Cats both said they endorse the CFL/CFLPA joint drug testing policy and accept the suspension . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;ALOUETTES REELING&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; Things have gone from bad to worse in Montreal this season . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Alouettes have a 3-9 record , have fired head coach Jacques Chapdelaine and defensive co-ordinator Noel Thorpe and have looked listless while losing their last five games . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The boo birds were out in full force last Sunday , when the Alouettes drew a decent crowd of over 22,000 to Percival Molson Stadium for a game against the Ottawa Redblacks. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Montreal wound up losing 29-11 , prompting one of their longest serving players to offer up an apology to the fans . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Defensive end John Bowman , who has been with the team since 2006 , tweeted : &amp;ldquo ; Merci/thanks to the 22k fans ( even the Ottawa fans , lol ) . I apologize from the bottom of my heart . We appreciate all of you guys . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; General manager Kavis Reed , who took over as head coach when Chapdelaine was fired , has a major task at hand to get this ship back on course . &lt;/p&gt; Postmedia NetworkWed , 20 Sep 2017 12:36:07 **45;32080;TOOLONG ...
